## scene-01-18 · A man and a woman sit facing each other at a table in a small apartment.

`PERFORMANCE — HER FACE. THIS IS THE PRIORITY OF THE SHOT.` · Close-up · Locked-off · 10s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-01-18](../shots/scene-01/18_a-man-and-a-woman-sit-facing-each-other-at-a-table.md), [scene-01-19](../shots/scene-01/19_scene-contexta-man-and-a-woman-sit-facing-each-oth.md)

````text
She is not composed and she is not hysterical. She is a woman saying something she has already been carrying for a long time, and the cost of saying it is continuously visible in micro-activity across her face. None of what follows is an "expression change" — it is the constant low-level noise of a real face under strain, running underneath everything.
````

## scene-08-02 · Continuation shot inside the same apartment.

`JAW SIGNAL` · Wide · Handheld · 25s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-08-02](../shots/scene-08/02_continuation-shot-inside-the-same-apartment.md), [scene-08-05](../shots/scene-08/05_continuation-shot-inside-the-same-apartment.md)

````text
The only physical sign of speech is at the jaw hinge and the throat, and it is subtle.
On stressed syllables, the padded gaiter shifts by one or two millimeters where it crosses the jaw hinge below the ear, and the fabric fold at the front of the throat moves slightly with the larynx.
The helmet itself does not nod, bob, or rock with the words. The head stays level.
No exaggerated jaw drop, no chewing motion, no bobbing head, no fabric pulsing in time with syllables.
````

## scene-10-07 · Close-up on an armored officer's face, seated.

`THE TWO ACTIONS — DIFFERENT GESTURES` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-10-07](../shots/scene-10/07_close-up-on-an-armored-officer-s-face-seated.md), [unfiled-project-root-08](../shots/unfiled-project-root/08_close-up-on-an-armored-officer-s-face-seated.md)

````text
They must not look like the same action twice.
THE HELMET is equipment: hardware, a catch, a cable, a seal. Unfastened in the correct order, by feel, with practised competence and one failed attempt. It resists, then releases suddenly. It comes off, and it is an object.
THE GAITER is clothing already on his body: nothing to unfasten, nothing to remove. It is only pulled down out of the way. Thick structured fabric that has to be worked past the nose and the chin, damp where it sat against his mouth, and it stays on him, bunched at the throat.
The first is a man dealing with his kit. The second is a man getting something off his mouth so he can breathe.
No dramatic reveal at either stage, no slow lift, no held moment as anything clears, no exhale timed to either action, no lowering anything reverently. He does both the way he would in a locker room.
````

## scene-10-07 · Close-up on an armored officer's face, seated.

`THE HANDS` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-10-07](../shots/scene-10/07_close-up-on-an-armored-officer-s-face-seated.md), [unfiled-project-root-08](../shots/unfiled-project-root/08_close-up-on-an-armored-officer-s-face-seated.md)

````text
The hands work as they have all through this sequence: no tremor, no hesitation, economical. What is different is that they are working on him, and it is fiddly and then it is physical.
Do NOT make the hands emotional. No shaking, no gripping hard, no clutching the helmet to his chest, no burying the face in them, no pressing them to the temples, no covering the eyes, no dragging a palm down the face after the gaiter comes down, no pushing the wet hair back off the forehead.
Once the gaiter is down the hands leave frame and do not return. They do not touch his face at any point.
````

## scene-10-09 · A man crosses an empty apartment from the sofa toward the kitchen doorway, weapon up, foll

`WHAT IS LEFT ON THE SOFA` · Wide · Handheld · 25s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-10-09](../shots/scene-10/09_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md), [scene-10-10](../shots/scene-10/10_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md)

````text
<<<prop_bear>>> and <<<prop_helmet>>> both stay on the sofa at screen-left for the entire take. Neither is touched, moved, picked up or looked at.
<<<prop_bear>>> sits where it was left: slumped against the sofa arm, at an angle, one arm caught behind it, exactly as in the reference.
<<<prop_helmet>>> sits on the seat beside it, where he put it down: resting on its side or upright on the cushion, cable trailing across the fabric, lens cluster dark and facing away. It has real weight and it has dented the cushion under it.
The two of them side by side on the sofa is a fact of the frame, not a composition. Neither is lit separately, neither gets an accent, neither is featured. He walks away from both of them and never looks back at them.
The camera does not linger on them, does not rack focus to them, does not return to them at the end.
Exactly one bear and exactly one helmet on the sofa. Nothing else on it.
````

## scene-10-09 · A man crosses an empty apartment from the sofa toward the kitchen doorway, weapon up, foll

`THE CRYING — HEARD, NEVER SEEN` · Wide · Handheld · 25s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-10-09](../shots/scene-10/09_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md), [scene-10-10](../shots/scene-10/10_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md)

````text
A baby is crying somewhere beyond the kitchen doorway. It is the only reason anything in this shot happens.
It is FAINT: thin, weak, muffled by distance and by walls, the cry of a newborn with very little strength in it. Not a loud healthy wail. It is at the edge of audibility and could almost be mistaken for the building.
It is INTERMITTENT. It comes in short broken bursts with gaps between them, and it does not run continuously under the shot.
It STOPS ONCE, in the middle of the take, for three full seconds, and then starts again. Nothing else changes in that gap.
It is never located precisely: it does not get clearly louder as he approaches and it does not clearly come from one direction. The room is hard to hear in.
The source is NEVER seen. No baby, no woman, no bundle, no movement, nothing revealed — not in the doorway, and not in the kitchen after the camera passes through the wall. Whatever is crying stays out of frame for all 25 seconds.
The crying is not treated as a musical cue. It does not swell, does not resolve, does not arrive on a beat, and nothing in the mix rises to meet it.
````

## scene-10-09 · A man crosses an empty apartment from the sofa toward the kitchen doorway, weapon up, foll

`THE WEAPON — UP, NOT AIMED` · Wide · Handheld · 25s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-10-09](../shots/scene-10/09_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md), [scene-10-10](../shots/scene-10/10_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md)

````text
He carries the carbine at the ready for the entire take: shouldered into the pocket of his shoulder or held high across his chest with both gloved hands, muzzle angled DOWN toward the floor.
It reads as a working posture, not as aggression. He is not aiming, not sighting, not sweeping the room with the muzzle. The muzzle stays low.
He does not fire, does not raise the muzzle to horizontal, does not point it at the doorway, does not point it at anything, including after he is in the kitchen.
The weapon moves with his body as he walks: real weight in his hands, the sling taking part of the load, the muzzle dipping and steadying with each step.
No LEDs, no illuminated optic, no laser, no weapon lamp, no emitted light of any kind from it.
````

## scene-11-15 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`HEIGHT AND EYELINE` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-11-15](../shots/scene-11/15_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md), [scene-11-16](../shots/scene-11/16_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
HE IS STANDING. SHE IS SEATED. That difference governs the frame and is never reduced.
Her head sits at roughly his chest height. She stays seated and never rises. He stands upright at full height and NEVER CROUCHES, KNEELS, SITS, STOOPS OR LOWERS HIMSELF, not even to take the wrist: the arm comes up to him, his hands reach down to it, and his body does not descend.
The camera sits low and close to her, at the height given by <<<image_1>>>, so the top frame line crosses his upper lip. It never rises, never tilts up, never goes looking for his eyes.
Her eyeline is UP AND SCREEN-RIGHT, past his visible mouth, to a face above the top edge of the picture, and she holds it there for the whole take.
````

## scene-11-15 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE FACE` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-11-15](../shots/scene-11/15_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md), [scene-11-16](../shots/scene-11/16_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
Her face is the <<<image_1>>> face and it does not change for a single frame: pale, drawn, exhausted, the mouth closed and still, the expression fixed. It is identical at 0:00, while she raises the arm, while he holds it, across the three seconds of the scan, and after the release.
Physically present and required: pallor under a faint damp sheen on the forehead, the nose and the upper lip; fine loose hair at the temple and along the jaw; breathing shallow and high in the chest, visible in the nostrils and at the open throat; a fine involuntary tremor at the jaw and in the muscle beside the mouth, small enough to almost miss; eyes locked high and not blinking longer than is comfortable; the eyes wet with strain, but no tear forms and nothing runs.
NOTHING ARRIVES ON THIS FACE at any point. No reaction when his hand closes, no reaction to the scanner, no reaction to the completion tone, no release of tension when he lets go, no exhale that reads as relief.
NOTHING READABLE PASSES OUT OF IT: no plea, no appeal, no apology, no defiance, no anger, no submission, no attempt to communicate.
Of him, only the beard, chin and closed mouth are visible, and they do not change either: no jaw set, no lip movement, no tightening, no speech.
````

## scene-11-15 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`MICRO-MOTION — NOTHING IS FROZEN` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s · **reused verbatim in 2 shots**

Used in: [scene-11-15](../shots/scene-11/15_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md), [scene-11-16](../shots/scene-11/16_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
Her head holds one angle for all ten seconds and is never frozen. Her rocking of the child travels up through her body and moves her head with it: a small slow oscillation, unchanging in speed and amplitude from the first frame to the last, continuing through the offer, the grip, the scan and the release. It never slows, never quickens, never pauses, and is never used as a reaction. Her shallow high breathing moves the head faintly on top of that, the two motions overlapping without falling into rhythm. There is a fine unsteadiness in the neck from holding the head back at a strained angle while carrying the child's weight: tiny corrections, late, overshooting by a hair and settling. Her eyes hold their high off-frame point through all of it, compensating exactly as real eyes do on a moving head.
Her held forearm keeps a fine tremor inside his grip across the three seconds and moves slightly with her rocking. This is involuntary and is not a gesture.
His standing column carries the slow low-frequency sway of a loaded body: the plate carrier lifting and settling with each breath, the ID placard swinging a millimetre, the slung <<<m7>>> moving with the chest as one mass, the chin and mouth at the top-right moving a hair with it. HIS TWO WORKING HANDS ARE STEADY in a way the rest of him is not: the scanner does not drift over the skin.
FORBIDDEN: no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no head movement that lands on an event, no movement timed to the scanner. No looping cycle, no smooth sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen head, no snapping between poses.
````

## intro-interview-02 · An elderly Japanese scientist is mic'd up by an unseen sound recordist in a white laborato

`EYELINE — THE CORE OF THE INTERVIEW REALISM` · Medium · Handheld · 22s

Used in: [intro-interview-02](../shots/intro-interview/02_an-elderly-japanese-scientist-is-mic-d-up-by-an-un.md)

````text
He is talking to a person, not to a camera, and this must be visible in every second of the take.
His eyeline sits on the INTERVIEWER, just off-lens to screen-right — a few degrees off the axis, close enough that we read his eyes fully, clearly not aimed down the barrel. He holds that eyeline as his home position and returns to it after every departure.
He NEVER looks into the lens. Not once, not on a strong line, not at the end. If his eyes cross the lens they are travelling, never landing.
Real interview eye behaviour, continuous and irregular:
— He holds the interviewer's eyes for most of the take, the steady contact of a man comfortable being questioned.
— Once inside the run his eyes go briefly down and to his own left for well under a second — the small involuntary disengagement of someone assembling a sentence. It falls MID-SENTENCE, never at a full stop, and he comes straight back to the interviewer.
— He glances once toward the tank at screen-left and back, at 0:05, before the cut.
— During the pause he stays on the interviewer. That one is deliberate.
This is not shiftiness and it is not evasion. It is a relaxed man conducting a conversation with one person in a room.
````

## intro-interview-03 · An elderly Japanese scientist, mic'd and standing in a white laboratory, finishes an answe

`EYELINE` · Medium · Handheld · 13s

Used in: [intro-interview-03](../shots/intro-interview/03_an-elderly-japanese-scientist-mic-d-and-standing-i.md)

````text
He is talking to a person, not to a camera, and this must be visible in every second of the take.
His eyeline sits on the INTERVIEWER, just off-lens to screen-right — a few degrees off the axis, close enough that we read his eyes fully, clearly not aimed down the barrel.
He NEVER looks into the lens. Not once, not on the final line, not at the end. If his eyes cross the lens they are travelling, never landing.
Once, mid-phrase in the first sentence, his eyes go briefly down and to his own left for well under a second and come back — the small involuntary disengagement of someone assembling a sentence.
DURING THE THREE-SECOND BEAT HE STAYS ON THE INTERVIEWER. He does not look away, does not look down, does not look at the tank. This is the most important eyeline instruction in the take.
On the final line his eyes stay on the interviewer throughout, and he holds them after it.
````

## intro-interview-03 · An elderly Japanese scientist, mic'd and standing in a white laboratory, finishes an answe

`RHYTHM` · Medium · Handheld · 13s

Used in: [intro-interview-03](../shots/intro-interview/03_an-elderly-japanese-scientist-mic-d-and-standing-i.md)

````text
— "But you can't legislate consumption. We tried, and nothing changed." is flat and unadorned. He does not perform the failure. It is a fact he reported a long time ago.
— The three-second beat is not filled. A man who has taught for thirty years is completely comfortable with a silence he has chosen, and comfortable holding another person's eyes through it.
— The final line is the slowest of all and evenly weighted. He is finishing an answer, not making a point.
Breath pattern: ordinary breaths at the ends of thoughts in the first sentence; one deep, audible, unhurried breath during the long beat; a normal breath before the final line. Breath falls at the end of a thought, never mid-clause.
Micro-variation: the two sentences do not share the same pace, volume or terminal pitch. Occasional very slight lengthening of a vowel where a non-native speaker naturally takes a fraction longer to place a word — never enough to read as hesitation.
He is patient rather than persuasive. Faintly weary of having to explain it again. Never enthusiastic, never salesmanlike, never conspiratorial, never confiding.
Lip movement matches the phonemes, phrasing and length of delivery exactly, with no exaggeration.
No subtitles, no captions, no on-screen text.
````

## intro-interview-03 · An elderly Japanese scientist, mic'd and standing in a white laboratory, finishes an answe

`HE DOES NOT LOOK AT THE TANK AT ANY POINT.` · Medium · Handheld · 13s

````text
The voice carries the settled authority of a senior academic, in fluent Japanese-accented English, clearly intelligible, never comic and never broken.
THE INTERVIEWER IS NEVER SEEN AND NEVER HEARD. No question audible, no murmur of acknowledgement, no shoulder, no shadow, no reflection, no figure at the edge of frame.
Exactly one person on camera. No other crew visible, no reflections of crew in the glass partitions or the tank glass.
The tank is in frame in the lower-left for the full 14 seconds.
No slate, no clapperboard, no timecode burn, no camera UI, no rec dot, no on-screen text, no subtitles, no captions.
Nothing in the room that is not in the location reference plus the plinth, tank, lavalier and cable.
The take ends on him waiting for the next question. Nothing is resolved, nothing is concluded, nothing lands.
````

## intro-stock-01 · A vote has just closed in a large international assembly chamber.

`HARD CUT AT 0:05` · Wide · Crane / high angle · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-01](../shots/intro-stock/01_a-vote-has-just-closed-in-a-large-international-as.md)

````text
SHOT B — 0:05 to 0:08
Camera 2. First frame already contains eight to ten seated delegates across two tiered rows, torsos facing forward toward the podium, and the screen-left aisle visible at the frame edge with one figure still walking away. No empty frame.
47° diagonal field of view, standard normal lens character, camera 9 meters from the nearest row, natural human-eye perspective, zero obvious distortion, comfortable depth of field, background rows readable but not compressed.
Delegate B is screen-centre in the near row, glasses now folded on the desk in front of him, right hand pressing the bridge of his nose, eyes closed, torso still facing forward.
Delegate C sits screen-right in the far row, head turned screen-left toward the delegate beside him, speaking two short quiet phrases with no audible words; the neighbour nods once and does not reply.
No delegate looks up at the display board. No delegate looks at camera.
Camera locked, no movement.
LENS CHECK SHOT B: 47° maintained, no drift, no telephoto compression.
````

````text
Camera 3. INSERT. First frame already contains a single delegate desk surface: forearm, wrist, sleeve, desk edge, gooseneck microphone base, a squared stack of papers, a folded pair of glasses. No empty frame, no macro abstraction.
29° diagonal field of view, short telephoto portrait lens character, camera 5 meters from the desk. Close framing achieved through lens reach, not physical proximity. The desk surface and hand are sharp, the rows behind begin to compress and soften, the subject pops clearly from the environment.

The watch face is plain and unreadable. No readable text on the papers, no nameplate lettering, no logos.
Camera locked, no movement.
````

## intro-stock-02 · A city square during a mass protest that has turned into a riot.

`OPERATOR AND CAMERA BODY — APPLIES TO ALL FOUR SHOTS` · Wide · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-02](../shots/intro-stock/02_a-city-square-during-a-mass-protest-that-has-turne.md)

````text
Shoulder-mounted broadcast camera, real mass on the operator's right shoulder, lens at standing eye height unless stated.
Handheld quality is professional and worked, not chaotic: a constant fine tremor from grip and forearm tension, footsteps absorbed by knees and shoulder so the vertical reads as a rolling weight shift rather than a drop, the operator's breathing visible as a slow low-frequency swell underneath the frame whenever he stands still.
The framing is actively fought for. The camera lags half a beat behind fast movement, then corrects and recovers the subject, with a small overshoot on each catch-up. The horizon drifts one or two degrees off level and is nudged back, oscillating around level rather than sitting crooked.
Focus is pulled manually and imperfectly: one visible hunt and recovery per shot, focus landing correctly within half a second.
Exposure is ridden manually: the iris opens and closes visibly when the frame swings between the bright overcast sky and the dark crowd, one correction per shot, never instant.
No digital jitter, no random shake, no gimbal smoothness, no drone float, no dolly or crane motion, no slow motion.

SHOT A — 0:00 to 0:04
Operator standing on the screen-left flank of the open ground, 12 meters back from the shield line, camera facing screen-right along the front. First frame already contains the crowd mass on screen-left, the open wet asphalt in midground, the shield wall on screen-right, and one burning container at the frame edge. No empty establishing frame, no reveal.
47° diagonal field of view, standard normal lens character, camera 12 meters from the shield line, natural human-eye perspective. Zero obvious distortion, natural body proportions, comfortable depth of field, the far side of the square readable but not exaggerated.
0:00 to 0:02 — The crowd surges screen-right as a mass. The front rank reaches the shields. The shield wall bends backward in a shallow curve and holds. The camera holds the geography wide, adjusting a few degrees screen-right to keep both lines in frame.
0:02 to 0:04 — Two thin arcs of smoke cross the open ground screen-left to screen-right. The camera lags, catches them late, and pans right to follow, overshooting slightly before settling.
LENS CHECK SHOT A: 47° maintained, no wide distortion, no telephoto compression.
````

## intro-stock-02 · A city square during a mass protest that has turned into a riot.

`HARD CUT AT 0:04` · Wide · Handheld · 15s

````text
SHOT B — 0:04 to 0:08
Operator now inside the crowd on the screen-left side, 6 meters back from the front rank, bodies pressing against him, camera facing screen-right toward the police line. First frame already contains shoulders, raised arms, and the shield wall visible through a gap between two bodies. No empty frame.
84° diagonal field of view, classic wide-angle lens character, camera 0.8 meters from the nearest body in frame. Foreground body presence looms larger than natural, environment remains visible to the frame edges, wide spatial context readable, camera physically close, deep edge-to-edge focus, no telephoto compression, no creamy portrait bokeh.
Handheld intensifies: the operator is knocked laterally by the mass, takes a stumble of half a step at 0:06, keeps the lens up and pointed screen-right throughout. He is being moved by the crowd, not choosing his footing.
The crowd wears scarves, hoods and cloth over their faces. Mouths open, shouting, no distinguishable words. Arms raised. One protester glances at the lens for under half a second and turns away.
0:04 to 0:06 — The crowd pushes forward one step and carries the operator with it.
0:06 to 0:08 — A wall of white gas smoke rolls in from screen-right at head height. Bodies turn away screen-left, hands to faces. The camera swings hard screen-left with the operator's turn, the frame smearing with motion blur before recovering.
LENS CHECK SHOT B: 84° maintained, camera stays physically close, no telephoto coverage.
````

## intro-stock-02 · A city square during a mass protest that has turned into a riot.

````text
SHOT C — 0:08 to 0:11
Operator crouched low at the screen-left edge of the open ground, camera braced at hip height, facing screen-right along the front line. First frame already contains wet asphalt in the near foreground, the burning fuel patch in midground, and the shield wall behind it. No empty frame.
29° diagonal field of view, short telephoto portrait lens character, camera 9 meters from the fire. Close framing achieved through lens reach, not physical proximity. The flame front and wet asphalt are razor-sharp, the shield line compresses closer behind them, the crowd flank behind that dissolves into soft bokeh, the fire pops clearly from the environment.
Handheld reads heavier and lower-frequency in the crouch: the operator is braced and breathing, small settling corrections, a slow drift screen-right pulled back once.
The burning fuel spreads flat across wet asphalt in a low sheet, flame height under half a meter, blue at the base, orange above, black smoke lifting straight up. Heat shimmer distorts the shield line seen through it.
The shield wall holds. Helmets and visors are uniform and anonymous. No insignia, no unit numbers, no readable text, no face visible behind any visor.
LENS CHECK SHOT C: 29° maintained, no wide-angle coverage, background stays compressed and soft.
````

## intro-stock-02 · A city square during a mass protest that has turned into a riot.

````text
Operator standing again on the screen-left flank, 15 meters back from the shield line, camera facing screen-right, higher and wider than Shot A to hold the whole confrontation. First frame already contains the crowd on screen-left, the open ground, the shield wall on screen-right, and heavy smoke in the upper third. No re-establishing move, no reframe to find the action.
47° diagonal field of view, standard normal lens character, camera 15 meters from the shield line, natural human-eye perspective, deep readable geography, no distortion.
0:11 to 0:13 — One bright point separates from the crowd mass on screen-left, travels in a flat parabolic arc across the open ground, and breaks on the asphalt 2 meters in front of the shield wall. Burning liquid spreads outward from the impact in an irregular pool. The camera catches the arc late, whips screen-right to follow it, and overshoots the impact by a few degrees before correcting back.
0:13 to 0:15 — The shield wall steps back one meter, reforms and holds. The crowd does not advance into the fire. Both masses stand still on either side of the burning ground. The camera settles, the horizon rocking slowly around level, the operator's breathing visible in the frame. He does not zoom in.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`REPORTAGE TREATMENT — THIS IS THE GOVERNING STYLE` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
This must read as news or documentary reportage: a single camera operator inside a real facility, recording what is happening because it is happening. Not stock, not cinema, not staged.
CAMERA: handheld, shoulder-carried, competent but unpolished. The operator is working fast and has no time to compose. Framing is functional and slightly imperfect: subjects a little off-centre, headroom sometimes tight, occasional small reframes as they correct on the fly. The horizon sits off level and stays there.
Movement is reactive, not designed: the camera adjusts to what people do, arriving a fraction late. Small drifts, small corrections, a shifting of weight. No dolly, no gimbal, no slider, no crane, no stabilised glide. Nothing rehearsed.
Occasional focus hunting: a brief hesitation before settling on a subject, corrected by hand. It reads as someone finding focus under pressure, not as a designed rack.
LIGHTING: available light only, and clearly so. Whatever the room has: overhead fluorescent, uneven, a bit too green, with bad falloff and some areas underlit. Nothing has been added, nothing has been shaped. No fill, no bounce, no key. Faces are lit badly the way faces in real institutions are lit badly.
COLOUR: uncorrected feeling. Slightly green from the fluorescents, mildly noisy in the shadows, not graded for beauty. It looks like a camera set to auto in a hard room.
EXPOSURE: broadly correct but not perfect. Slight over on the pale table sheet, slight under in the corners. Small auto-exposure adjustments as the framing changes.
PRESENCE: the people are aware a camera is in the room the way people in a working facility are — nobody performs for it, but nobody is unaware of it either. Occasional glances toward the lens that do not linger. The operator does not have privileged access; some angles are partly obstructed by a shoulder, a post, a door frame.
FORBIDDEN: tripod stillness, clean symmetrical framing, designed camera moves, shaped lighting, cinematic contrast, film emulation, slow motion, anything that suggests the scene was set up for the camera.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`NO DUPLICATION — ABSOLUTE` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
The subject requires several people being processed in turn, and that is exactly the setup that generates clones. The solution is structural: the sequence shows DIFFERENT WRISTS rather than different faces. Only two faces appear in the whole sequence, in separate shots, and they are never in frame together.
EVERY PERSON IS A DIFFERENT INDIVIDUAL. No two people share a face, a build, a hairstyle, a skin tone, an age or an item of clothing. No twins, no repeated extras, no cloned figures, no mirrored bodies, no identical people at different distances.
NINE PEOPLE EXIST IN TOTAL and no more. Each shot contains only the people specified for it, and most contain one or two. Do not add background figures to fill any shot.
NO ARCHITECTURAL REPETITION in any shot: no receding corridor, no vanishing point, no repeating bays, no row of identical doors, no grid of identical windows, no rank of identical light fittings extending into distance, no rows of identical cubicles or chairs stretching away.
NO EXTERIOR anywhere: no window, no view out, no city, no buildings, no towers, no skyline. Every shot is enclosed and shallow, with a wall, screen or surface closing the depth within a metre or two.
Forbidden throughout: tiling, fractal repetition, mirrored halves, symmetrical duplication, repeated furniture into distance, repeated posters or signage, repeated people at diminishing scale.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`CODE ORIENTATION — PERPENDICULAR TO THE ARM` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
The block of bars sits ACROSS the forearm, not along it.
THE LONG AXIS OF THE BLOCK runs perpendicular to the long axis of the arm: it goes from one side of the forearm to the other, across the width, at ninety degrees to the line running from elbow to hand.
THE INDIVIDUAL BARS run parallel to the arm — each bar points along the forearm, from elbow toward wrist.
Read with the arm extended and the palm up, the block reads as a wide short rectangle lying across the limb, like a band segment, and the bars are scanned across the width of the arm.
It is NEVER rotated ninety degrees so the block runs lengthwise up the forearm. It is never diagonal. It is never vertical.
It sits just below the wrist crease, on the soft inner skin, and curves with the roundness of the forearm — the bars at the edges of the block foreshorten where the arm turns away.
This orientation is identical on every person in the sequence.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`THE NINE PEOPLE — EACH ONE DIFFERENT` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
Only two are ever seen as a FACE. The rest are forearms, hands or cropped partial bodies, which is what keeps them distinct.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`SEEN AS FACES` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
1. WOMAN AT THE STATION: thirties, dark hair pulled back, plain grey coat over a jumper, one sleeve pushed to the elbow. Shot 4 only. Head turned away from her own arm.
2. OLDER WOMAN WAITING: seventies, white hair in a thin bun, brown wool coat buttoned to the neck, ticket in both gloved hands. Shot 5 only, alone.
SEEN AS FOREARMS ONLY — four different wrists in Shot 3, each clearly a different person:
3. A young man's forearm: lean, tanned, dark hair on the arm, a cheap watch pushed above the elbow.
4. An older man's forearm: thin, pale, loose skin, prominent veins and tendons, liver spots.
5. A young woman's forearm: slight, very pale, a faded old scar on the inner side, no hair.
6. A heavy middle-aged forearm: broad, ruddy, dry skin, a wedding ring on the hand.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`SEEN AS PARTIAL BODIES ONLY` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
7. THE OPERATOR: a woman of about forty in pale hospital scrubs, blue nitrile gloves, hair tied back, paper mask pulled down under her chin. Seen from the shoulders down and from behind. Her face is never shown.
8. A PERSON IN THE QUEUE: torso and hands only at the belt barrier in Shot 1, dark coat, holding a ticket. Cropped at the neck, no face.
9. A SEATED PERSON: legs and lap only in Shot 2, dark trousers, hands with a ticket. Cropped at the chest, no face.
Nobody else appears in any shot. No second operator, no security, no children, no other staff, no crowd.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`WHAT IS HAPPENING — A ROUTINE HEALTH PROGRAMME` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
This plays as a public vaccination and registration drive. Everything about it is orderly and administrative.
There is a queue behind a belt barrier. There are numbered tickets. There is hand sanitiser. The operator wears scrubs and gloves and works with the unhurried competence of someone on a long shift.
People push their own sleeves up. Nobody is restrained, held down or forced. Nobody resists, cries or pleads. There are no guards, no uniforms beyond scrubs, no weapons, no restraints, no shouting.
The people waiting are neutral and mildly bored, the way people are at a clinic.
The tattooing is presented as the record-keeping half of a medical procedure: the vaccination and the mark that proves it, done at the same table by the same person.
That ordinariness is the entire point, and reportage treatment is what delivers it. Nothing in the coverage suggests this is remarkable — it is simply being filmed.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`THE PROCEDURE` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
The operator works on one inner left forearm at a time, positioning each code exactly as specified in the BIRTHCODE LOCK and CODE ORIENTATION blocks.
She wipes the area, positions a stencil, and works the compact grey device across the skin in short passes. A thin buzz, on and off, with pauses as she wipes with gauze.
Between people: she strips her gloves, drops them in the bin, pumps sanitiser, pulls on fresh gloves, and the next forearm is laid on the paper sheet.
<<<birthcode>>> is left in the skin, identical in design, position and orientation on every arm, with the surrounding skin slightly reddened from the needle. No blood pooling, no dripping, no gore. It is a tattoo, not an injury.
She does not look at anyone's face. She looks at the arm.

SHOT 1 — THE QUEUE LINE (0:00 to 0:03)
Handheld medium close on the retractable belt barrier: the woven belt between two posts, a hand resting on it, a paper ticket in the other hand. TORSO AND HANDS ONLY, cropped at the neck, no face.
A plain wall a metre behind closes the frame. No queue receding, no other people.
The framing is slightly off-centre and the horizon a degree or two off level. One small reframe as the hand moves. Focus is already there.
The ticket is turned once. The belt hangs slack.
Off-screen: the tattoo device buzzing in short bursts, a sanitiser pump.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`HARD CUT.` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
SHOT 2 — WAITING (0:03 to 0:06)
Handheld close on a seated person's lap: dark trousers, two hands, a paper ticket with a two-digit number. Cropped at the chest, no face.
Beside them one empty plastic chair. A plain wall close behind. NO ROW OF CHAIRS RECEDING.
The operator has crouched for this and the frame drifts slightly as they settle. Focus hunts briefly on the ticket before landing.
The hands are still. One thumb moves across the ticket edge. Nothing else.
The buzz continues off-screen.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`HARD CUT.` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
SHOT 3 — FOUR WRISTS (0:06 to 0:12)
The central shot. Handheld close on the station table from a steep angle over the operator's shoulder: the paper sheet, her blue-gloved hands, the device. Part of her shoulder intrudes at one frame edge — the operator is filming past her, not from her position.
SIX SECONDS, in which FOUR DIFFERENT FOREARMS pass through the frame. Arms arrive, are worked on briefly, and leave; the next arrives. The camera holds roughly on the table and drifts a little, correcting late each time a new arm comes in.
The four are clearly different people — tanned and hairy, thin and spotted, pale and scarred, broad and ruddy with a wedding ring — legible in skin, age, hair and hand.
Between each: gloves stripped into the bin, a pump of sanitiser, fresh gloves pulled on. That changeover marks one person ending and the next beginning.
On each arm: a wipe, one short pass of the device, the buzz, a wipe. <<<birthcode>>> is left on each, identical in design, position and orientation — the block lying ACROSS the forearm below the wrist crease, bars running along the arm — on four visibly different skins.
NO FACES in this shot. No shoulders in frame except the operator's obstructing edge, no heads. Only forearms, hands, gloves, table and device.
Focus hunts once, on the second arm, and recovers.
Exposure slightly hot on the white paper sheet.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`HARD CUT.` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
SHOT 4 — THE WOMAN (0:12 to 0:15)
Handheld close on the woman at the station. Her face, three-quarter, head turned away from her own arm.
She is neutral: a person having something done to her arm at a clinic and not watching it. She blinks. Nothing on her face. Once, briefly, her eyes flick toward the lens and away again — she knows the camera is there and it is not interesting to her.
Her arm is out of frame below. The operator is out of frame. Nobody else in shot.
Background: the wall and the table edge, soft.
The framing is a little tight on headroom. One small correction mid-shot.
The buzz continues, close. It stops once and starts again. She does not react.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`HARD CUT.` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [intro-stock-04](../shots/intro-stock/04_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-vaccination-and.md)

````text
SHOT 5 — THE OLDER WOMAN (0:15 to 0:17)
Handheld medium close on the older woman waiting, ALONE. An empty chair on one side, nobody else in frame.
She holds her ticket in both gloved hands on her lap, buttoned to the neck, looking straight ahead. Patient and neutral.
Then, without looking down, she begins to work one glove off her hand, slowly. That is all.
A plain wall close behind. No depth, no other chairs, no other people.
The camera is slightly low and off to one side, as though the operator stepped in without asking. The frame drifts a little.
The buzz continues off-screen, faint.
````

## intro-stock-04 · Reportage footage from a hospital vaccination and registration point.

`HARD CUT.` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

## intro-stock-05 · Reportage footage from a large crowded hospital hall during a mass vaccination and registr

`REPORTAGE TREATMENT — THIS IS THE GOVERNING STYLE` · Wide · Handheld · 8s

Used in: [intro-stock-05](../shots/intro-stock/05_reportage-footage-from-a-large-crowded-hospital-ha.md)

````text
This must read as news or documentary reportage: a single camera operator inside a real facility, recording what is happening because it is happening. Not stock, not cinema, not staged.
LIGHTING: available light only, and clearly so. Overhead fluorescent, uneven, a bit too green, with poor falloff and some areas underlit. Nothing added, nothing shaped, no fill, no bounce, no key.
COLOUR: uncorrected feeling. Slightly green from the fluorescents, mildly noisy in the shadows, not graded for beauty. A camera set to auto in a hard room.
EXPOSURE: broadly correct but not perfect. Slight under in the deeper parts of the hall, slight over on pale surfaces. Small auto-exposure adjustments as the framing shifts.
PRESENCE: the operator is IN the crowd and does not have privileged access. Bodies pass between the lens and the subject. Shoulders and backs obstruct the frame. Nobody clears a path for the camera.
FORBIDDEN: tripod stillness, clean symmetrical framing, designed camera moves, shaped lighting, cinematic contrast, film emulation, slow motion, anything that suggests the scene was arranged for the camera.
````

## intro-stock-05 · Reportage footage from a large crowded hospital hall during a mass vaccination and registr

`NO DUPLICATION — ABSOLUTE, AND THE METHOD FOR ACHIEVING IT` · Wide · Handheld · 8s

Used in: [intro-stock-05](../shots/intro-stock/05_reportage-footage-from-a-large-crowded-hospital-ha.md)

````text
A crowd of this size is exactly what produces cloned faces and repeated bodies. Instructing against it is not enough, so the coverage is built to make it impossible:
NO FACE IN THIS SEQUENCE IS EVER RESOLVED. Not one. The crowd is filmed so that faces are unavailable: heads seen from behind, bodies cropped at the neck by the frame edge, faces turned away, faces occluded by other bodies, faces too small or too soft to read. If no face is ever legible, no face can be repeated.
The crowd reads as MASS, not as individuals: coats, shoulders, the backs of heads, hair, bags, sleeves. Texture and colour vary constantly across it — different coat colours, different hair, different heights, different postures — so it reads as many people, but no single person is ever a subject.
The camera is LOW and BEHIND shoulders, so the near bodies fill the lower half of frame and block the sightlines that would otherwise show rows of faces.
There is ALWAYS an out-of-focus foreground body: someone close to the lens, soft, cutting into the frame, breaking up the space behind them.
NO ARCHITECTURAL REPETITION: no receding corridor, no vanishing point down the middle of the room, no rank of identical light fittings extending away, no grid of identical windows, no row of identical doors, no repeating columns, no repeating identical counters stretching into the distance.
NO EXTERIOR: no window, no view out, no city, no buildings, no towers, no skyline.
Forbidden throughout: tiling, fractal repetition, mirrored halves, symmetrical duplication, repeated furniture into distance, repeated posters or signage, repeated people at diminishing scale, any two people sharing a face, a build, a hairstyle or an item of clothing.
````

## intro-stock-05 · Reportage footage from a large crowded hospital hall during a mass vaccination and registr

`THE HALL — LARGE, WIDE, NOT DEEP` · Wide · Handheld · 8s

Used in: [intro-stock-05](../shots/intro-stock/05_reportage-footage-from-a-large-crowded-hospital-ha.md)

````text
A very large hall inside a hospital, a converted assembly space or concourse, taken over for a mass programme. High ceiling. Pale institutional walls, scuffed. Worn vinyl floor.
ITS SIZE READS LATERALLY, NEVER IN DEPTH. The hall extends well beyond both frame edges: the crowd continues off-screen left and off-screen right in every shot, so the room is clearly bigger than what is being filmed. That is how the scale is communicated.
IT DOES NOT RECEDE. A wall closes the depth roughly ten metres behind the crowd, and it is visible in both shots. There is no far end disappearing into distance, no vanishing point, no perspective funnel, no space that goes on forever ahead of the lens.
Along one side, a run of service counters with glazed screens, each visibly different: different heights of paperwork, different clutter, one shutter half down, one opening unmanned. NOT identical booths in a row.
Loose plastic chairs across the floor in irregular groups, facing different directions, some pulled out of line, several occupied and several empty. Never in neat repeating rows.
Retractable belt barriers on posts marking queue lanes, belts slack, one dropped on the floor.
A paper ticket dispenser. A small digital number display high on the wall.
ROUGHLY 150 PEOPLE in frame across the hall, and more implied beyond the edges: standing, sitting, waiting, shuffling. Coats on, bags held, tickets in hands. Busy and slow.
Nothing else: no posters, no notices, no plants, no clock, no signage, no branding, no readable text anywhere.
````

## intro-stock-05 · Reportage footage from a large crowded hospital hall during a mass vaccination and registr

`WHAT IS HAPPENING — A ROUTINE HEALTH PROGRAMME` · Wide · Handheld · 8s

Used in: [intro-stock-05](../shots/intro-stock/05_reportage-footage-from-a-large-crowded-hospital-ha.md)

````text
This plays as a mass public vaccination and registration drive. Everything about it is orderly and administrative.
People queue. They hold numbered tickets. They wait, shift their weight, look at nothing. Nobody is restrained, herded, pushed or forced. There are no guards, no uniforms except hospital scrubs behind the counters, no weapons, no barriers beyond the retractable belts.
The mood is BOREDOM, not fear. This is a long wait in a public building.
That ordinariness is the entire point.

SHOT 1 — THE HALL (0:00 to 0:04)
Handheld wide from inside the crowd, camera at chest height, in among the standing people rather than above them.
The frame is WIDE ACROSS: the hall fills the width of the shot and continues past both edges, packed with people. A plain wall closes the depth about ten metres back and is visible. No corridor, no vanishing point.
In the near foreground, out of focus and cutting into the lower third: the shoulder and back of the head of someone standing directly in front of the lens. They do not move away.
Beyond them, the crowd: a hundred or more people visible, all seen as backs of heads, shoulders, coats and bags. Some standing, some seated in the irregular chairs. Not one face is resolved — the ones facing this way are too soft, too small, or turned. Every colour of coat is different. Every head is different.
Movement is slow and ambient: a few people shuffle forward, one steps sideways out of frame, someone shifts a bag from one hand to the other. No choreography, no synchronised movement, nobody crossing the frame dramatically.
The camera drifts laterally and corrects late, once. Focus is on the middle distance and hunts briefly at 0:02 before settling.
The frame is level and stays level; the drift is lateral, not rotational.
````

## intro-stock-05 · Reportage footage from a large crowded hospital hall during a mass vaccination and registr

`HARD CUT.` · Wide · Handheld · 8s

Used in: [intro-stock-05](../shots/intro-stock/05_reportage-footage-from-a-large-crowded-hospital-ha.md)

````text
SHOT 2 — THE QUEUE (0:04 to 0:08)
Handheld medium, camera at chest height, positioned INSIDE the queue lane looking along it toward the counters.
The queue runs at an ANGLE across the frame, not straight away from the lens: it enters from one side, crosses the middle ground, and reaches the counters at the far side of frame. This angle is what prevents a receding line of repeating figures.
Behind and beyond the queue, more of the hall and more of the crowd fill the frame and continue past both edges. The closing wall is visible behind them.
In the near foreground, out of focus: the back of the person immediately ahead in the queue, filling one side of the frame, coat and shoulder and the back of a head. They occupy a third of the image and block the sightline down the line.
Beyond them, twenty or more people in the queue, seen from behind and at three-quarter rear: backs, shoulders, hands holding tickets, one arm scratching a neck. NOT ONE FACE resolved.
At the far side, one counter opening, and behind the glazed screen a pair of hands in blue nitrile gloves working on a forearm laid on the counter — small in frame, partly obstructed, not the subject. Whoever's arm it is, no face is shown.
The queue moves ONCE, a small collective shuffle forward of half a step, uneven and untidy: some people move, some do not, some move a beat later than others. Never in unison.
The camera is jostled slightly as the person in front moves, and reframes late. Focus stays on the middle of the queue.
The frame is level and stays level; the drift is lateral, not rotational.
END.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`REPORTAGE TREATMENT — THIS IS THE GOVERNING STYLE` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
This must read as news or documentary reportage: a single camera operator inside a real hospital, recording what is happening because it is happening. Not stock, not cinema, not staged, not a promotional health film.
LIGHTING: available light only, and clearly so. Overhead fluorescent, uneven, slightly green, with poor falloff and some areas underlit. Nothing added, nothing shaped, no fill, no bounce, no key, no warm glow over the cots.
COLOUR: uncorrected feeling. Slightly green from the fluorescents, mildly noisy in the shadows, not graded for beauty and specifically not warmed. A camera set to auto in a hard room.
EXPOSURE: broadly correct but not perfect. Slight over on the white bedding, slight under in the corners. Small auto-exposure adjustments as the framing shifts.
PRESENCE: the operator does not have privileged access. Angles are partly obstructed. The nurse works past the camera without accommodating it.
FORBIDDEN: tripod stillness, clean symmetrical framing, designed camera moves, shaped lighting, cinematic contrast, film emulation, slow motion, soft warm nursery lighting, anything tender in the treatment, anything that suggests the scene was arranged for the camera.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`NO DUPLICATION — ABSOLUTE, AND THE METHOD FOR ACHIEVING IT` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
A row of identical cots is exactly the geometry that produces tiling and repetition. The coverage is built to prevent it:
THE ROW IS FILMED ACROSS, NEVER DOWN. The line of cots runs from one side of the frame to the other, roughly parallel to the lens, seen from the side. It NEVER runs away from the camera toward a vanishing point. No perspective funnel, no diminishing repetition into distance.
EXACTLY SIX COTS are in frame in the wide shot, and no more. The row continues past both frame edges by implication, but only six are visible and each one is individually different: different blanket colour and pattern, one cot with a folded cloth over the end rail, one with its side rail down, one with a small chart clipped to it, different amounts of bedding, slightly different angles to the row.
The cots are ALIGNED BUT NOT IDENTICAL. They are the same type of trolley but visibly used differently.
NO ARCHITECTURAL REPETITION: no corridor, no vanishing point, no rank of identical light fittings receding, no grid of identical windows, no row of identical doors, no repeating columns. A wall closes the depth about four metres behind the cots and is visible.
NO EXTERIOR: no window, no view out, no city, no buildings, no daylight.
THE NEWBORNS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE. Each visible one differs: different amounts of dark hair, different skin tone and blotching, different position — one on its side, one with an arm out of the swaddle, one with a hat on, one turned away and barely visible. No two are the same baby.
NO ADULT FACE IS EVER RESOLVED. The nurse is seen from behind, from the side, cropped at the neck, or with her back to the lens. Her face is never legible in any shot.
Forbidden throughout: tiling, fractal repetition, mirrored halves, symmetrical duplication, repeated furniture into distance, repeated bodies at diminishing scale, any two cots or two babies that look the same.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`CODE ORIENTATION — PERPENDICULAR TO THE ARM` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
The block of bars sits ACROSS the forearm, not along it.
THE LONG AXIS OF THE BLOCK runs perpendicular to the long axis of the arm: from one side of the forearm to the other, across the width, at ninety degrees to the line running from elbow to hand.
THE INDIVIDUAL BARS run parallel to the arm — each bar points along the forearm, from elbow toward wrist.
With the arm extended and the palm up, the block reads as a wide short rectangle lying across the limb, like a band segment, and the bars are scanned across the width of the arm.
It is NEVER rotated ninety degrees so the block runs lengthwise up the forearm. It is never diagonal. It is never vertical.
It sits just below the wrist crease and curves with the roundness of the tiny forearm — the bars at the edges of the block foreshorten where the arm turns away.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`THE PEOPLE` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
THE NURSE: a woman of about forty in pale hospital scrubs, blue nitrile gloves, hair tied back. Seen from behind, from the side, or cropped — her face is never resolved in any shot. She works along the line with the unhurried competence of someone on a long shift.
THE NEWBORNS: six visible in the wide shot, each different as specified above. In Shot 3, one newborn's forearm only, no face.
Nobody else. No second nurse, no doctor, no parents, no visitors, no security, nobody in a doorway.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`WHAT IS HAPPENING — A ROUTINE CHECK` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
The nurse walks the line, checking each newborn briefly: a look, a hand adjusting a blanket, a glance at a chart clipped to a cot. It is a round, done many times a shift.
Nothing is dramatised. Nobody is distressed. No baby is removed, lifted out, taken away, or handled roughly. No parent is present and none is implied on camera.
The mood is administrative, not sinister and not tender. The room is doing its job.
That ordinariness is the entire point.

SHOT 1 — THE ROW (0:00 to 0:05)
Handheld wide, camera at chest height, positioned to one side of the row and looking ALONG THE FRONT of it, so the cots run left to right across the frame at a shallow angle. They are seen from the side, at similar scale, NOT receding away from the lens.
SIX COTS in frame, each individually different as specified. A wall closes the depth about four metres behind them. The row implies more beyond both frame edges.
In each cot, a newborn: different hair, different skin tone, different position, one with a hat, one turned away, one with an arm free of the swaddle.
The nurse enters from one side and moves along the line away from the camera's near edge, seen from behind and three-quarter rear. She stops at the second cot, leans in, adjusts a blanket, and moves on. Her face is never visible.
The camera drifts laterally and corrects late once as she moves. Focus is on the middle of the row and hunts briefly at 0:02 before settling.
Level frame, upright, staying upright.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`HARD CUT.` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
SHOT 2 — THE NURSE AT A COT (0:05 to 0:09)
Handheld medium, camera slightly behind and to the side of the nurse. Her back and one shoulder fill part of the frame, partly obstructing the view — the operator is filming past her.
She is bent over one cot, both gloved hands working: she straightens a swaddle, touches the back of a tiny hand, checks something at the cot's end rail. Practical, quick, unremarkable.
The newborn is visible below her hands but partly obscured by her arms: a small face turned to one side, eyes closed. Only the one baby is in frame, and one neighbouring cot at the edge, empty of a visible occupant.
Her face is never resolved: the camera is behind her shoulder and she never turns toward the lens.
Slight reframe as she shifts her weight, arriving late. Exposure adjusts slightly on the white bedding.
Level frame, upright, staying upright.
````

## intro-stock-06 · Reportage footage from a hospital nursery.

`HARD CUT.` · Wide · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [intro-stock-06](../shots/intro-stock/06_reportage-footage-from-a-hospital-nursery.md)

````text
SHOT 3 — THE WRIST (0:09 to 0:12)
Handheld extreme close-up on a newborn's forearm and hand, lying on the bedding. Palm up, inner forearm toward the lens.
The frame holds the tiny hand, the wrist and the forearm, with a band of white cot bedding around it. No face, no other body part, no nurse, no cot rail — just the arm and the sheet.
<<<birthcode>>> is clearly visible on the inner wrist, positioned and oriented exactly as specified: the block lying ACROSS the width of the forearm just below the wrist crease, bars running along the arm, settled blue-grey ink, no numerals, scaled to the tiny limb.
The skin around it is normal newborn skin: blotchy, red-pink over pale, fine downy hair, translucent with faint blue veins beneath. No redness around the code, no swelling — it healed days ago.
The hand moves once: the fingers uncurl slowly and unevenly in sleep, thumb last, then half-curl again. That is the only movement.
Focus is on the wrist and holds. Shallow depth of field, the bedding softening away immediately behind.
The camera floats slightly with the operator's breathing and corrects once.
Level frame, upright, staying upright.
END.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`TWO SECONDS PER SHOT — ONE FACT EACH` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
Each shot is two seconds long. That is enough time for ONE piece of visual information and no more. Each shot is built around a single fact and does not attempt a second.
Nothing develops within a shot. Nothing arrives, nothing resolves, nothing is set up and paid off. The camera does not have time to find anything and does not try.
The sequence reads as fragments of a much longer rush — pieces cut out of the middle of things, none of them complete.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`REPORTAGE TREATMENT — THIS IS THE GOVERNING STYLE` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
News footage from a single shoulder-mounted broadcast camera and one operator, caught in the square across the same few minutes. The five shots are edit points in the same rushes.
THE OPERATOR IS IN IT, NOT WATCHING IT. He is in the open with everyone else, exposed, moving, giving ground. He does not have a clear view and never gets one.
CAMERA: handheld, shoulder-carried, working hard and losing things. Framing is fought for and often wrong: subjects off-centre or half out of frame, the camera arriving after the event rather than before it.
THE HORIZON STAYS LEVEL. No dutch angles, no canted frames, no crooked horizons at any point. The chaos is in the movement, the occlusion and the subject, never in the tilt.
Focus is pulled manually and imperfectly: at these durations there is one hunt per shot and it does not always land before the cut.
Exposure is ridden manually and lags: the iris arrives late when the frame swings between the bright smoke-lit sky and dark bodies.
No dolly, no gimbal, no slider, no crane, no drone, no stabilised glide, no designed move, no slow motion.
TEXTURE: broadcast video, not film. Clean in the centre, softer at the edges, natural video contrast, clipping on the sky and the fire, compression breakup during the fastest movement, faint horizontal smear on strong motion.
No film grain, no halation, no CCTV timestamp, no phone-video framing, no rolling-shutter jelly, no VHS artefacts, no found-footage aesthetic, no colour grade, no teal-and-orange.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`WHAT MAKES THIS A CIVIL CONFLICT AND NOT A RIOT` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
THERE IS NO FRONT. This is the single most important thing about the sequence.
There is no police line, no shield wall, no cordon, no formation, no two masses facing each other. Nobody is holding a position.
People move in CONTRADICTORY DIRECTIONS across the same ground: some running one way, some the other, some standing still in the middle of it, some walking. In every shot at least two people are moving in opposite directions past each other. Nobody is being led.
It is impossible to tell from the footage who is on which side, or whether there are sides. Nobody is uniformed except in fragments — a helmet here, a vest there, mixed in with people in ordinary coats. Nothing identifies anyone.
The square is OPEN and the space is not controlled. There is no barrier, no line, no boundary anywhere in the sequence.
This is the aftermath state of a place where the structure has already failed, not a confrontation between two organised bodies.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`NO DUPLICATION — ABSOLUTE` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
NO FACE IS EVER RESOLVED in any shot. Faces are covered, turned away, cropped by the frame edge, obscured by smoke, or moving too fast against a dark background.
NOBODY IS SEEN WHOLE. Bodies are cropped, cut off by other bodies, or partly hidden by smoke. No complete figures standing clear.
NO ROWS, NO RANKS, NO FORMATIONS. Nobody is arranged, nobody is evenly spaced, nobody is standing in a line. This is guaranteed by the fact that there is no front.
DEPTH IS SHORT: smoke closes off everything beyond fifteen or twenty metres in every shot. No far crowd receding, no background mass of small figures, no vanishing point.
NO ARCHITECTURAL REPETITION: no rank of identical facades, no repeating identical windows, no colonnade, no row of identical lamp posts, no receding street. What is visible of the buildings is fragmentary and close, and mostly lost in smoke.
No two people share a build, a coat, a hood or a posture.
Forbidden throughout: tiling, fractal repetition, mirrored halves, symmetrical duplication, cloned bodies, repeated figures at diminishing scale, background crowds made of repeated small figures, ranks of identical uniformed figures.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`THE SQUARE` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
A large open paved city square. Wet stone underfoot with standing water. Debris scattered across it: broken paving, a fallen barrier, scattered paper, a burnt-out bin.
Three or four fires burning at different points across the ground: low, flat, spreading with the camber — burning debris and burning fuel, nothing above waist height. Black smoke lifting from each and shearing sideways, joining into a low drifting layer across the whole square.
Buildings enclose the square but are only fragmentarily visible: a stretch of stone facade, a doorway, part of an upper storey. Everything beyond twenty metres is lost in smoke.
Nothing else. No vehicles, no barriers, no lines, no structures erected by anyone.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`NO SPECTACLE` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
Forbidden absolutely: explosions, fireballs, blast waves, burning people, vehicles on fire, artillery, muzzle flashes, tracer, aircraft, gunfire, coloured smoke, smoke grenades, flares.
No blood, no visible wounds, no bodies on the ground, no injured being carried, no arrests, no beatings, no weapons of any kind visible in frame.
The violence has already happened somewhere else and is not shown. What is shown is people moving through the state it left behind.

SHOT 1 — CROSSING (0:00 to 0:02)
Handheld, camera at chest height, in the open square. Bodies cross close to the lens in both directions — one passing left to right in the near foreground, blocking the frame for half a second, another going right to left further out.
Behind them, smoke and a low fire. Nothing beyond fifteen metres.
The camera is knocked slightly as the near body passes and reframes late.
Level frame. Focus hunts and does not fully land before the cut.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`HARD CUT.` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [intro-stock-07](../shots/intro-stock/07_a-city-square-during-a-civil-conflict-no-police-li.md)

````text
SHOT 2 — THE FIRE (0:02 to 0:04)
Handheld, camera lower, closer to the ground. A flat fire burning across the wet stone fills the middle of frame, low and spreading, orange with blue at the base. Black smoke lifts straight up and shears.
A figure walks past behind it, seen through the heat shimmer, unhurried and unidentifiable, and is gone.
The camera drifts. Exposure closes down late as the flame brightens in frame.
Level frame.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

`HARD CUT.` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

````text
SHOT 3 — STANDING STILL (0:04 to 0:06)
Handheld, camera turned to a different part of the square. In the middle of frame, three or four people are STANDING STILL in the open, doing nothing, while others move past them in both directions.
They are not a group and not facing the same way. They are simply stopped, in a place where everyone else is moving.
Smoke drifts across them and half obscures them.
The camera holds roughly on them and drifts. Focus hunts once.
Level frame.
````

## intro-stock-07 · A city square during a civil conflict. No police line, no formation, no front — people mov

````text
Handheld, camera swinging late to follow someone running screen-right, catching them already half out of frame and never quite centring them.
A second person crosses screen-left in front of the lens as the camera swings, blocking it completely for a fraction of a second.
Everything behind is smoke.
The camera overshoots and corrects back.
````

````text
Handheld, camera stopped, breathing hard. The frame is mostly drifting smoke, lit flat by the sky, with the vague dark shapes of people moving through it at fifteen metres — unidentifiable, unresolvable, going in different directions.

The camera floats with the operator's breathing and does not move.
````
