## scene-10-09 · A man crosses an empty apartment from the sofa toward the kitchen doorway, weapon up, foll

`THE WALL PASS — A FALSE FLAT` · Wide · Handheld · 25s

Used in: [scene-10-09](../shots/scene-10/09_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md)

````text
Between 0:16 and 0:20 the camera follows him through the wall that divides the room from the kitchen.
This must read as a deliberate device, not as a glitch or a ghost effect. The wall behaves like a FALSE FLAT on a set: as the camera advances, the section of wall between the room and the kitchen slides cleanly out of frame to one side, and the camera continues forward into the kitchen without interruption.
It is a physical, staged move: the wall leaves, the space continues. The camera does not fade through it, does not dissolve, does not become transparent, does not phase through solid matter, does not clip through geometry.
There is no visual effect on it: no ripple, no glow, no warp, no morph, no digital transition, no distortion at the edge, no light change as it passes.
Nothing about the man acknowledges it. He does not react, does not look at the wall, does not slow. He simply walks into the kitchen and the camera comes with him.
Nothing about the sound acknowledges it either: no whoosh, no sweep, no cue. Only the room acoustic changes, because the camera is now in a smaller tiled room.
After the pass, the room he came from is behind the camera and out of frame. The sofa, the bear and the helmet are not seen again.
````

## scene-10-10 · A man crosses an empty apartment from the sofa toward the kitchen doorway, weapon up, foll

`THE WALL PASS — A FALSE FLAT, NOT THE DOORWAY` · Wide · Handheld · 25s

Used in: [scene-10-10](../shots/scene-10/10_a-man-crosses-an-empty-apartment-from-the-sofa-tow.md)

````text
This is the controlling instruction for the last third of the take. The man and the camera do not use the same opening.
HE uses the doorway. The doorway is at screen-right, past the dark cabinet. He walks to it, goes through it side-on and leaves frame. He is out of shot from that moment until the wall is gone.
THE CAMERA DOES NOT USE THE DOORWAY. It does not turn toward it, does not track to it, does not aim at it, does not pass through it, does not follow him into it. It never travels toward screen-right at all.
Instead, from 0:16, the camera advances STRAIGHT FORWARD along its own axis, into the blank section of the dividing wall in front of it — a solid stretch of white-green distempered wall well to the LEFT of the doorway, with no opening in it, the section the man never touched.
That blank section behaves like a FALSE FLAT on a set: as the camera reaches it, the flat slides cleanly out of frame to one side, laterally and in one continuous move, and the kitchen is simply there behind it. The camera keeps advancing forward without slowing and without changing direction, and ends up inside the narrow tiled kitchen.
It is a physical, staged move: the wall leaves, the space continues. The camera does not fade through it, does not dissolve, does not become transparent, does not phase through solid matter, does not clip through geometry, does not squeeze through the doorway at the last moment.
There is no visual effect on it: no ripple, no glow, no warp, no morph, no digital transition, no distortion at the edge, no light change as it passes.
Nothing about the man acknowledges it. He is already out of frame when it starts, and when he reappears in the kitchen he does not react, does not look at the wall, does not look at the camera. He does not know a wall has left.
Nothing about the sound acknowledges it either: no whoosh, no sweep, no cue, no sliding-flat noise. Only the room acoustic changes, because the camera is now in a smaller tiled room.
The two routes must read as separate: he went through a door, the camera went through a wall. They arrive in the same kitchen from different lines.
After the pass, the room he came from is behind the camera and out of frame. The sofa, the bear and the helmet are not seen again.
````

## scene-11-01 · A woman sits on the edge of the bed with her back to him, holding something against her ch

`WHAT SHE IS HOLDING — NEVER SHOWN` · Wide · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [scene-11-01](../shots/scene-11/01_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-with-her-back.md)

````text
She has something in her arms, held against her chest, and her body is curled over it.
It is NEVER seen. Her back is to the camera and her arms and torso hide it completely. No part of it enters frame: no bundle, no cloth, no limb, no hand, no face, nothing.
She does not lift it, does not adjust it, does not shift it into view. It stays hidden for all 10 seconds.
The audience infers it from the shape of her body and from the sound. That is all they get.
````

## scene-11-01 · A woman sits on the edge of the bed with her back to him, holding something against her ch

`SHE DOES NOT TURN` · Wide · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [scene-11-01](../shots/scene-11/01_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-with-her-back.md)

````text
Her back is to the camera for the entire take. She never turns around, never looks over her shoulder, never shows her face, never stands.
She knows he is there. The floorboards announced him. She has heard the room change. And she does not turn.
Her only movements are: the small continuous rise and fall of holding a body against her own chest, one very slight tightening of the arms at 0:04, and a single small turn of the HEAD a few degrees to the side at 0:07 — not enough to show her face, not enough to look at him, just enough to angle an ear back toward the room. Then it comes back.
That head movement is the entire acknowledgement she gives. Nothing else.
No flinch, no jump, no gasp, no scramble, no pleading, no protective sudden movement, no dramatic reaction of any kind.
````

## scene-11-01 · A woman sits on the edge of the bed with her back to him, holding something against her ch

`WHAT THE CAMERA DOES NOT DO` · Wide · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [scene-11-01](../shots/scene-11/01_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-with-her-back.md)

````text
It does not look down at his own hands or weapon. It does not check the corners of the room. It does not glance at the window or the door. It does not scan.
It does not tilt down her body. It does not try to see around her to what she is holding. It does not lean.
It does not push in during the hold. It does not tighten. It does not drift closer.
There is no blink effect, no eye-closing black, no breathing vignette, no heartbeat pulse, no focus-pull to simulate looking. He is looking at one thing and the shot stays there.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`NO SUDDEN MOVEMENT — ABSOLUTE` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
Nothing <<<mum>>> does in this take is quick. Not one movement.
Every action she makes has a full arc: it starts slowly, travels slowly, decelerates and settles. Nothing begins abruptly and nothing stops dead. There is no snap, no jerk, no flinch, no startle, no twitch, no whip, no sudden correction anywhere in her body at any point — including when she sees him.
This is physical: she is holding a sleeping newborn against her chest and any fast movement would wake it. Her whole body has been calibrated for hours to avoid exactly that.
THE HEAD TURN is the point of greatest risk and must be the slowest thing in the shot: a full second and a half of continuous rotation, even in speed, decelerating into its final position. No snap round, no whip, no head jerking up, no double-take.
Her shoulders do not jump. Her arms do not tighten suddenly. Her hands do not clutch. Her breath does not catch sharply. Her eyes do not widen quickly. Seeing him changes nothing about the speed of her body.
The rocking is continuous and even throughout and never changes tempo.
Forbidden everywhere: startle reactions, flinches, gasps, sharp intakes of breath, sudden protective clutching, snapping the head round, jolting the body.
The same applies to him: <<<char_cap2>>> walks slowly and evenly and never moves quickly at any point.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`THE MOVE — AROUND HER, ALONG THE FOOT OF THE BED` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
The camera travels a slow arc around her, on foot, following the line of the foot of the bed.
It starts BEHIND her: her back and the back of her head fill the frame, the curtained window beyond her shoulders as a soft bright rectangle. Whatever she is holding is completely hidden by her body.
It ends at roughly THREE-QUARTER BACK: about 70 degrees of arc, no more. From there her near cheek, jawline and ear read, the far eye is visible past the bridge of her nose, and the wrapped bundle in her arms is uncovered and clearly visible against her chest.
THE 70-DEGREE LIMIT IS FIXED. The camera never goes further round, never reaches profile, never reaches frontal. The window must stay behind her for the whole take.
The arc is at a constant radius of roughly 1.5 metres and a constant height at her seated eye level. The camera does not get closer, does not rise, does not drop.
The move runs from 0:01 to 0:07 and then stops. From 0:07 the camera is static for the rest of the take.
It is a real walking arc: uneven footfalls dropping the frame vertically, framing drifting and eased back, corrections arriving a fraction late. Not a dolly, not a gimbal glide, not a smooth mechanical orbit.
Its speed matches hers. It is slow enough that it does not feel like an arrival.
CRITICALLY: the final camera position must place the open doorway BEHIND HER AND IN FRAME, over her far shoulder, softly, so that when he walks through it he is visible without the camera moving.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`THE REVEAL — THE BABY COMES OUT FROM BEHIND HER` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
For the first second, from directly behind, the child is completely hidden.
As the camera comes round, it is uncovered gradually and by geometry alone: first the edge of the linen wrap past her arm, then the curve of the wrapped head, then the small face against her chest. By 0:07 it is fully visible.
Nothing marks this. No focus rack to it, no push-in, no light change, no sound. It is simply progressively less hidden as the angle changes.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`HE PASSES THROUGH — HE DOES NOT STOP` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
At 0:08, behind her and out of focus, <<<char_cap2>>> appears in the open doorway and walks through it.
He is walking slowly, at the same unhurried pace he has held all through this sequence, weapon up in both gloved hands with the muzzle angled DOWN. Not aiming, not sighting, not sweeping the room.
HE DOES NOT STOP. He crosses the doorway from one side to the other over about three seconds and continues out of the far side of the opening, out of frame. He does not enter the bedroom, does not pause in the doorway, does not turn toward her, does not look at her, does not raise the weapon, does not speak.
He is checking rooms. He has not reached this one yet. Whether he saw her is not answered.
By 0:13 the doorway is empty again.
He never becomes aware of the camera and never looks toward it.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`THE TURN AND THE FOCUS CHANGE` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
At 0:09, while he is mid-crossing, she turns her head toward the doorway.
The turn is SLOW: her head comes round over a full second and a half, off the baby and back over her shoulder, decelerating into position. Not a snap, not a startle, no flinch, no gasp.
Her body does not stop. The rocking continues at exactly the same tempo. The thumb keeps moving on the cloth. She turns her head toward him and goes on soothing her child at the same time.
She does not stand, does not shrink back, does not turn her body away, does not shield the baby, does not pull it tighter. Her arms do exactly what they were already doing.
THE FOCUS CHANGE happens from 0:10.5 to 0:12. The focal plane leaves her and travels back to him in the doorway. She and the baby go fully soft in the foreground — an unreadable pale mass with a dark head — and he resolves out of the blur: the plate carrier, the weapon, the bare face, moving slowly across the opening.
It is a real hand-pulled rack, taking about a second and a half, with one small overshoot and correction as it lands on him.
Because of the long-ish lens and the distance, she becomes genuinely soft. This is intended. Do not keep her sharp, do not split the difference, do not use a deep focus that holds both.
FOCUS DOES NOT COME BACK TO HER. It stays on the doorway for the rest of the take, including after he has gone.
````

## scene-11-02 · A woman sits on the edge of a bed with her back to the room, holding a newborn against her

`THE BABY — ASLEEP, AND STAYS ASLEEP` · Wide · Handheld · 14s

Used in: [scene-11-02](../shots/scene-11/02_a-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-a-bed-with-her-back-to.md)

````text
<<<char_baby>>> is asleep and does NOT wake up at any point in this take.
Its eyes stay closed. It does not cry, does not scream, does not wail.
The only movements: the small rise and fall of shallow newborn breathing under the cloth, one brief unconscious twitch of the escaped fist, one small mouth movement without waking.
It makes almost no sound: at most one small snuffle, once, early in the take.
Crucially, NOTHING happens to it when he appears or when she turns. It sleeps through the entire thing, unaware. It never cries at the moment he crosses.
The baby is never handled roughly, never lifted, never repositioned dramatically, never shown in distress. Its face is slack and blotchy and asleep.
````

## scene-11-04 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`THE WORD` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-04](../shots/scene-11/04_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
He says one word and one word only: "Go."
It is spoken at 0:03. Nothing is said before it and nothing after it.
DELIVERY: flat, quiet, unraised — his own voice in the room, not through a helmet pickup. Low and dry. No emphasis, no urgency, no kindness, no gentleness, no weight placed on it. No rising inflection. It is not whispered, not hissed, not growled, not choked.
It is said in exactly the register he used for procedure earlier in the film: the voice of a man giving an instruction, applied to the opposite of a procedure. He is not making a gesture and he is not offering anything. He is telling someone to leave.
He is looking down at her as he says it.
He does not repeat it. He does not add anything. He does not say her name, does not say "now", does not say "quickly", does not say anything else.
The word is short and it is over almost before it registers. It does not land on a beat and nothing in the shot marks it.
LIP SYNC: his mouth is bare and visible, and the word is formed clearly on it — a single syllable, the lips opening and closing once. The gaiter is at his throat and does not cover his mouth. At the three-quarter profile angle the mouth is foreshortened, so the movement must be unmistakable: the word must read as his and not as something said off-frame.
Voice matched to <<<audio_1>>>.
````

## scene-11-04 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`HE LETS HER PAST — A SMALL, UNWATCHED MOVEMENT` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-04](../shots/scene-11/04_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
Because he is standing this close to her, she cannot get out without him moving. He moves.
It is SMALL: a half-step back and a rotation of the shoulders to open the space beside him, the way a man steps aside in a narrow corridor. Not a stride, not a retreat, not a flourish. Roughly thirty centimetres.
It is UNWATCHED. By the time she is rising his eyes have already gone level, and they stay there. He does not turn his head to see her coming, does not look down at her again, does not check the gap. The movement is automatic and slightly late — he shifts because a body is arriving, not because he has decided to be kind.
No gesture accompanies it: he does not raise a hand, does not usher her, does not gesture toward the door, does not hold anything open, does not nod her through.
His weapon does not move: no lifting it out of the way, no clearing it, no adjusting.
He returns to roughly where he was within a second of her passing, without looking at where she has gone.
This is courtesy from the body only, and the face does not authorise it. That contradiction is the point.
````

## scene-11-04 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`SHE RISES AND PASSES` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-04](../shots/scene-11/04_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
Between 0:05 and 0:07 <<<mum>>> stands up beside him and leaves.
The dark mass at the bottom of frame lifts: she stands, slowly, and comes up past him. For a moment she fills the near foreground completely, fully out of focus, and occludes him almost entirely. Then she clears and is gone out of frame, and he is there again, with empty room behind him.
She is CLOSE to the lens and therefore FULLY OUT OF FOCUS: a large soft pale mass with the smaller pale shape of the bundle held against her. Her face is never legible. The baby is never legible.
She does not stop. She does not look back at him once she has stood. She does not speak, does not thank him, does not hesitate, does not touch him. She stands and goes at a steady unhurried pace.
She does not run and she does not hurry. She is carrying a sleeping newborn and she moves the way she has moved all through this sequence.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

Neither head is frozen. Both carry continuous involuntary micro-motion, the motion of a real head on a real neck, and it never stops from the first frame to the last. A head held perfectly rigid reads as a still image with eyes blinking on it and is wrong.

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`HIS HEAD` · Close-up · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [scene-11-07](../shots/scene-11/07_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
It is heavy, tilted down a few degrees, and held in that position by neck muscle that is already tired. It drifts and is corrected, continuously, in movements of one or two degrees.
His hard breathing drives it: the head lifts fractionally on each inhale and settles on each exhale, in the same fast shallow uneven rhythm as the breathing, never in a clean cycle. The jaw and the throat move visibly under the gaiter with it.
The weight of the plate carrier and the rifle transmits up through his torso: a slow low-frequency sway of the whole standing body, a centimetre at most, carrying the head with it in a slow irregular figure that never repeats.
Every twenty to forty frames there is a tiny postural correction — the head drifting slightly off its held angle and being pulled back — arriving late and overshooting by a hair before settling. These are muscular, not decisions.
The head keeps a fixed AVERAGE angle across the whole take: the drift happens around that angle and never travels away from it. His eyeline stays on her face throughout and the micro-motion never carries his gaze off her.
On the line at 0:02 the jaw opens and closes on the four words and the head moves a little with the articulation, as any speaking head does. It does not nod on the words, does not punctuate them, does not dip at the end of the sentence.
FORBIDDEN FOR HIM: no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no cocking of the head, no turn, no lift of the chin, no dropping of the head as a beat, no head movement that lands on an event — nothing on the line, nothing when her arm comes up, nothing when he takes it, nothing when the tone sounds, nothing on the release. The head is never used to react to anything.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`HER HEAD` · Close-up · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [scene-11-07](../shots/scene-11/07_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
It is turned and tilted up toward his face and stays there. Her micro-motion is smaller and quieter than his: slower drift, less amplitude, fewer corrections. She is hunched and holding weight, and her body is more settled than his.
Her continuous rocking of the child travels up through her spine and moves her head with it, a small slow unchanging oscillation. That oscillation never changes speed or amplitude at any point and is never used as a reaction.
Her breathing moves her head faintly, shallower and more even than his.
FORBIDDEN FOR HER: no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no head lowering, no head turning away, no head movement on the line, on the grip, on the tone or on the release.
BOTH HEADS: the two motions are independent and unsynchronised. They never fall into rhythm with each other and never mirror each other. Neither head ever moves in a way that could be read as an answer to the other.
No perfectly smooth motion, no looping cycle, no sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen head, no snapping between poses, no head movement driven by the camera's movement.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`THE LINE` · Close-up · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [scene-11-07](../shots/scene-11/07_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
He says one line and one line only: "Give me your hand."
It is spoken at 0:02. Nothing is said before it and nothing after it, by anyone.
DELIVERY: flat, quiet, unraised — his own voice in the room, not through a helmet pickup. Low and dry. No emphasis, no urgency, no threat, no kindness, no gentleness, no weight placed on it. No rising inflection. It is not whispered, not hissed, not growled.
It is said in exactly the register he used for procedure earlier in the film: the voice of a man giving an instruction he has given a thousand times. It is not a demand and not a request. It is the next step.
He is looking down at her face as he says it.
He does not repeat it. He does not add anything. He does not say "now", does not say "please", does not say her name, does not say anything else.
NO GESTURE ACCOMPANIES THE LINE. He does not extend a hand, does not turn a palm up, does not indicate her arm, does not nod toward it, does not lean in.
LIP SYNC: his mouth is bare and visible, and the four words are formed clearly on it. The gaiter is at his throat and does not cover his mouth. At the three-quarter profile angle of <<<image_2>>> the mouth is foreshortened, so the articulation must be unmistakable: the line must read as his and not as something said off-frame.
Voice matched to <<<audio_1>>>.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`HE ASKS, SHE COMPLIES` · Close-up · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [scene-11-07](../shots/scene-11/07_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
He asks for the hand. He does not take it before asking, does not reach out, does not gesture toward her arm, does not point.
Then he waits. Two full seconds pass between the line and her movement, and in those two seconds he does nothing at all: no repetition, no impatience, no gesture, no leaning in, no shift of weight, no action of any kind beyond breathing, one blink and the involuntary head micro-motion.
She complies. The movement is SLOW and steady — she is still holding a sleeping newborn against her chest with the other arm, and she does nothing quickly. The arm comes out and up from below the frame, into the lower part of the shot, inner side up, and stops. She holds it out toward him.
It is not a plea and not a defiance. She has been through this procedure and she knows what it is. She presents the arm the way she would present a document.
Her arm and wrist stay OUT OF FOCUS for the whole take, in the lower part of frame: a pale soft shape, unreadable. THE BIRTH CODE ITSELF IS NEVER LEGIBLE: no readable mark, no numbers, no glyphs, no barcode resolved at any point.
She does not speak. She does not look away from him. Her other arm keeps holding the child and her small rocking never stops.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

`THE SCAN — HEARD AND READ OFF THE ARM, NEVER SEEN` · Close-up · Handheld · 12s

Used in: [scene-11-07](../shots/scene-11/07_three-quarter-profile-close-up-on-a-man-s-face.md)

````text
The mechanics follow <<<image_3>>> exactly, and all of it happens below the bottom edge of frame except her forearm.
At 0:06 his gloved hand closes around her wrist, BELOW THE FRAME EDGE, at the position shown in <<<image_3>>>. The grip itself is not visible.
THE SCANNER IS NEVER SEEN. No device enters frame, no reader, no housing, no screen, no lens, no beam, no projected grid. His free hand going to his belt happens entirely below the bottom edge. Nothing of the instrument is ever visible in any frame.
HIS HANDS ARE NEVER SEEN. Neither hand enters the frame at any point in the take.
WHAT IS VISIBLE IS ONLY HER FOREARM, out of focus in the lower part of frame, and it carries the entire procedure. Its movement is driven by <<<image_3>>>:
— at 0:06 the forearm stops moving and steadies as it is taken, the tremor of holding it up replaced by the stillness of being held;
— at 0:07 the forearm is rotated inner-side-up by the amount shown in <<<image_3>>>, and drawn down toward the bottom edge by the distance shown there, as the scanner is brought to it;
— from 0:07 to 0:11 the forearm is held rigid in the position shown in <<<image_3>>>, moving only with her breathing and her rocking, while the scan runs along it;
— at 0:11 the forearm is released and falls away under its own weight, out of the lower frame.
THE GRIP IS FUNCTIONAL AND STATIC. It closes once and does not change. No regrip, no adjustment, no tightening, no thumb movement, no stroking, no rubbing, no shift of the fingers at any point across the hold. Whatever his hand is doing below the frame, it is holding a limb still for a machine and nothing else.
The scan lasts FOUR FULL SECONDS. Nothing else happens in them. Nobody moves. Nobody speaks.
He does not lift the arm to his own eyeline. He does not turn it to look at it. The rotation is a small working adjustment done by feel, not an inspection.
HE NEVER LOOKS AT THE WRIST. His eyes stay on her face for the entire procedure: not down when she offers the arm, not when he takes it, not while the scan runs, not on the release. His head never turns or dips toward it. He does not check the scanner. He does not look at a readout.
That is the point of the shot. The procedure runs correctly and he is not watching it.
At 0:11 he lets go. The hand simply opens: not lowering her arm, not placing it down, not pushing it away. Her arm falls away and does not come back.
He does not hold on afterwards. He does not squeeze before releasing. He does not pat it, does not touch her again, does not make any gesture. His free hand returns the scanner to his belt below frame, unseen and unhurried, and stops there.
````

## scene-11-07 · Three-quarter profile close-up on a man's face.

````text
Read from <<<image_1>>>: their gazes cross. She is looking UP at him; he is looking DOWN at her.
HIS EYELINE: down and slightly across, onto her FACE, at a steep close-range angle because he is standing and she is sitting. The head is tilted down a few degrees; the eyes carry most of it.
The gaze target does not move at any point in the take. Not on the line, not on the offer, not on the grip, not across the four seconds of the scan, not on the release. The head's involuntary drift moves around that target and the eyes compensate for it, exactly as real eyes hold a fixed point on a moving head.
HER EYELINE: up at his face, and she does not look away either — not when he speaks, not when she offers the arm, not while the scan runs, not when he releases her. Her eyes likewise hold him through her own head's small motion.
Nothing readable passes between them in either direction: no plea, no reassurance, no apology, no thanks, no defiance. Neither nods. Neither signals.
Neither looks into the lens at any point.
````

No readable text anywhere in frame: no signage, no labels, no on-screen text, no legible writing on the ID placard, no interface, no HUD.
She does not stand and does not leave in this take.
<<<char_baby>>> stays asleep, is never touched, adjusted, uncovered or repositioned, and makes no sound at any point.

She is frightened and she is not showing it. The fear is in the body, not in the performance.
Physically present and required: pallor under a damp sheen; damp strands of dark hair stuck across her forehead and one cheek, matching the sweat and disorder on him in <<<image_2>>>; eyes fixed and unblinking longer than is comfortable, then a fast blink, then fixed again; breathing shallow and high in the chest, visible in the nostrils and at the throat under the fleece collar; a fine involuntary tremor at the jaw and in the muscle beside the mouth, small enough to almost miss; the eyes wet with the strain of not blinking, but no tear forms and nothing runs; the mouth closed, lips slightly parted only enough to breathe.
Her face does not change across the five seconds. Whatever is on it at 0:00 is on it at 0:05. Nothing arrives, nothing resolves, nothing escalates.
NOTHING READABLE PASSES OUT OF THIS FACE: no plea, no appeal, no apology, no defiance, no anger, no submission, no attempt to communicate. She is not asking him for anything. She is waiting.

## scene-11-10 · Close-up on the woman, seated, her head lowered over the child.

`HIS ENTRANCE` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-10](../shots/scene-11/10_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-her-head-lowered-over.md)

````text
He walks in from the LEFT EDGE at an ordinary unhurried walking pace, one step, then another, and stops. It takes roughly one and a half seconds from the first sliver of him at the edge to fully stopped.
He crosses close to the lens, so he enters as a large soft dark mass that grows across the left of frame: first a blurred edge, then the shoulder, then the collar and the back and side of his head, filling the left third to two-fifths and arriving exactly at the <<<image_2>>> position.
He arrives with WEIGHT: the mass decelerates over the last half-step rather than stopping dead, and there is one small settle of the plate carrier and the body as the weight comes down on the final step. Then he is still.
He stops IN FRONT OF HER, one step away, facing her, his head already angled down toward her when he arrives. He does not turn to find her after stopping.
HE NEVER CROSSES HER. He does not pass in front of her face, does not occlude her at any point, does not continue toward frame right. He stops at the left, in his own third of the frame, and her face is never blocked.
His face is never legible at any point of the entrance: the angle keeps the back and side of his head to camera and he stays far in front of the focal plane.
FORBIDDEN DURING THE ENTRANCE: no lunge, no rush, no looming toward the lens, no whip of movement, no dramatic shadow sweeping across her, no camera flinch, no rack focus onto him, no pause mid-step, no reframing to make room for him.
````

## scene-11-10 · Close-up on the woman, seated, her head lowered over the child.

`THE LIFT` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-10](../shots/scene-11/10_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-her-head-lowered-over.md)

````text
She raises her head AFTER he has stopped, not while he is moving. There is a short beat of stillness between his arrival and her movement, and in that beat nothing happens.
It takes roughly one second and it is SLOW, EVEN AND CONTINUOUS. One movement, no stages, no hesitation, no pause halfway, no false start, no small drop before it begins.
It starts from the neck, not the shoulders: her torso does not rise, her spine does not straighten, her shoulders do not square. Only the head comes up.
The chin travels up and slightly toward screen-left, the throat opening and lengthening as it goes. The face rotates into the key from screen-right and becomes legible for the first time in the shot: the brow, then the eyes, then the full face, then the catchlight.
Her eyes arrive on him at the very end of the movement, not before it: she does not look up first and lift the head after. The gaze arrives with the face.
It does not overshoot and it does not settle back. It arrives at the <<<image_2>>> head position and stops there, held, with only involuntary micro-motion after.
Her loose hair moves with delay and settles late; the fine strands at her temple and jaw trail behind the movement and come to rest across her cheek.
The rocking of the child does not pause, slow or quicken during the lift. The two motions are independent.
FORBIDDEN DURING THE LIFT: no snap, no jerk, no sudden turn, no dramatic reveal, no rise timed to a sound, no eyes opening as if waking, no hair pushed back, no breath taken before it, no exhale after it, no expression changing as the face comes into view.
````

Her head is never frozen, before the lift or after it. It carries continuous involuntary micro-motion for all five seconds, and that micro-motion is separate from the lift.
Her rocking of the child travels up through her spine and moves her head with it: a small slow oscillation, unchanging in speed and amplitude from the first frame to the last. ## scene-11-11 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the bed, the newborn cradled in her arms, her head lowere

`THE LINE` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-11](../shots/scene-11/11_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-bed-the-newbor.md)

````text
She says one word and one word only: "Coglione."
It is spoken at 0:03, after he has stopped and after her eyes are already on him. Nothing is said before it and nothing after it, by anyone. He does not answer.
LANGUAGE: Italian. The word is "coglione", three syllables, co-glio-ne, the middle syllable carrying the stress, the "gli" as the Italian palatal lateral.
DELIVERY: flat, quiet and low. Almost under the breath, but fully voiced and clearly audible — not a whisper, not hissed, not muttered into her chest. It is said straight at him.
IT IS NOT SHOUTED AND IT IS NOT SPAT. No volume, no venom, no snarl, no tremor, no crack in the voice, no rising inflection, no emphasis placed on any syllable beyond the natural stress of the word. It is not an insult thrown; it is a word said out loud by someone who has stopped bothering not to say it.
It comes out of the breathing she is already doing. She does not draw breath to say it and does not exhale after.
It is exhausted rather than angry: the flatness of someone with nothing left, not the heat of someone losing their temper.
LIP SYNC: her face is sharp and frontal enough that the articulation must be exact and unmistakable. The lips round on the first syllable, the jaw opens on the stressed middle syllable, the mouth closes to a small neutral shape on the last. The word must read as hers and not as something said off-frame.
Her eyes stay on him for the whole word and after it. She does not look away as she says it and does not look away when it is finished.
She does not repeat it. She adds nothing. Nothing else is said by anyone at any point.
````

## scene-11-11 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the bed, the newborn cradled in her arms, her head lowere

Used in: [scene-11-11](../shots/scene-11/11_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-bed-the-newbor.md)

````text
SHE RAISES HER HEAD BECAUSE HE ARRIVES. The movement begins a fraction of a second after the first of him crosses the frame edge, and her eyes arrive on him at the moment he comes to rest. The two movements overlap: she is coming up while he is still coming in.
It takes roughly one and a quarter seconds and it is CONTINUOUS. Not slow and not fast: the speed of a head coming up because something has entered the room, without hurry and without alarm. One movement, no stages, no pause halfway, no false start.
IT IS NOT A STARTLE. No snap, no jerk, no whip round, no flinch preceding it, no shoulders jumping. Her body does not move: her torso does not rise, her spine does not straighten, her shoulders do not square, her arms do not tighten on the child. Only the head comes up.
The chin travels up and slightly toward screen-right, the throat opening and lengthening as it goes. The face rotates into the key from screen-left and becomes legible for the first time in the shot: the brow, then the eyes, then the full face, then the catchlight.
Her eyes arrive on him at the very end of the movement, not before it: she does not look up first and lift the head after. The gaze arrives with the face.
It does not overshoot and it does not settle back. It arrives and stops, held, with only involuntary micro-motion after.
Her loose hair moves with delay and settles late; the strands falling across the side of her face trail behind the movement and come to rest across her cheek and jaw, CLEAR OF HER MOUTH.
The rocking of the child does not pause, slow or quicken during the lift. The two motions are independent.
FORBIDDEN DURING THE LIFT: no gasp, no intake of breath, no dramatic reveal, no rise timed to a sound, no eyes opening as if waking, no hair pushed back, no expression changing as the face comes into view, no mouth opening in anticipation of the line.

HE NEVER CROSSES HER. He does not pass in front of her face, does not occlude her at any point, does not continue toward frame left. He stops in his own third of the frame and her face is never blocked. THE WEAPON NEVER CROSSES HER EITHER: no part of it and no part of its sling passes in front of her or points at her.
FORBIDDEN DURING THE ENTRANCE: no lunge, no rush, no looming down toward her, no crouching on arrival, no whip of movement, no hand going to the weapon, no raising or swinging of the weapon, no muzzle sweeping through frame, no dramatic shadow sweeping across her, no camera flinch, no rack focus onto him or onto the weapon, no pause mid-step, no reframing to make room for him, and above all NO TILT UP TO FIND HIS FACE.
````

## scene-11-13 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the edge of the bed, the newborn cradled in her arms, her

`THE FACE` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-13](../shots/scene-11/13_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-edge-of-the-be.md)

````text
She is frightened. The fear is present in the body and is never played on the face.
Her face is the <<<image_2>>> face and it does not change for a single frame of this take: pale, drawn, exhausted, the mouth closed and still, the expression fixed. It is identical before he enters, while he walks in, and after he has stopped.
Physically present and required: pallor under a faint damp sheen on the forehead, the nose and the upper lip; loose strands of hair across the side of her face and jaw, clear of the eyes; breathing shallow and high in the chest, visible in the nostrils and at the open throat above the fleece collar; a fine involuntary tremor at the jaw and in the muscle beside the mouth, small enough to almost miss; eyes locked high on a point above the frame and not blinking longer than is comfortable; the eyes wet with strain, but no tear forms and nothing runs.
NOTHING ARRIVES ON THIS FACE WHEN HE ARRIVES. No recognition, no fear surfacing, no tightening, no micro-expression on the footfalls or on the stop. The event happens beside her and her face does not report it.
NOTHING READABLE PASSES OUT OF THIS FACE: no plea, no appeal, no apology, no defiance, no anger, no submission, no attempt to communicate. She is not asking him for anything. She is waiting, and she was already waiting before the shot began.
She does not look away for a single frame. She does not lower her gaze, does not look at the child, does not look at the weapon, does not look into the lens.
````

## scene-11-13 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the edge of the bed, the newborn cradled in her arms, her

`HEAD MOVEMENT — LIVING, NOT STILL` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-13](../shots/scene-11/13_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-edge-of-the-be.md)

````text
Her head holds one angle for all five seconds, and that head is never frozen: it carries continuous involuntary micro-motion from the first frame to the last.
Her rocking of the child travels up through her body and moves her head with it: a small slow oscillation, unchanging in speed and amplitude throughout. It never slows, never quickens, never pauses, and is never used as a reaction.
Her shallow high breathing moves the head faintly on top of that, faster and less regular than the rocking, the two motions overlapping without falling into rhythm.
There is a fine unsteadiness in the neck from holding the head at a raised, slightly strained angle while carrying the child's weight: tiny corrections, arriving late, overshooting by a hair and settling. It is present from the first frame, not something that begins when he arrives.
Her eyes hold their high off-frame point through all of it, compensating for the movement exactly as real eyes do on a moving head. THE MICRO-MOTION NEVER CARRIES HER GAZE OFF SCREEN-RIGHT, NEVER LOWERS IT, AND NEVER READS AS A LOOK.
His column, once he has stopped, carries the involuntary motion of a standing body under load: the slow low-frequency sway of the mass, the plate carrier lifting and settling with each breath, the pouches shifting a millimetre, the slung weapon resting against the carrier and moving with it as one mass, the chin and beard edge at the top of frame moving a hair with it. It is never a gesture, never a turn, and it never descends toward her. His jaw never opens.
FORBIDDEN: no lift, no lowering, no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no head turning away, no chin lift, no cocking, no head movement that lands on anything or that coincides with his footfalls or his stop. No looping cycle, no smooth sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen head, no snapping between poses.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`HEIGHT AND EYELINE` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-14](../shots/scene-11/14_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
HE IS STANDING. SHE IS SEATED. That difference governs the frame and is never reduced.
Her head sits at roughly his chest height. She stays seated and never rises. He stands upright at full height and never crouches, kneels, sits, stoops or lowers himself, not even to take the wrist: he reaches down to her, the arm comes up to him, and his body does not descend.
The camera sits low and close to her, at the height given by <<<image_1>>>, so the top frame line crosses his upper lip. It never rises, never tilts up, never goes looking for his eyes.
Her eyeline is UP AND SCREEN-RIGHT, past his visible mouth, to a face above the top edge of the picture. She holds that eyeline for the whole take.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`SCREEN DIRECTION — ABSOLUTE` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-14](../shots/scene-11/14_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
— <<<mum>>> stays on the SCREEN-LEFT side of frame for the whole take.
— <<<char_cap2>>> stays in the SCREEN-RIGHT foreground for the whole take. He does not cross, does not move, does not exit.
— Her gaze goes UP AND SCREEN-RIGHT throughout and never points screen-left, never lowers.
— Her forearm comes up from the bottom of frame into the space BETWEEN them, in the lower middle, and is worked on there.
— The key stays off-frame SCREEN-LEFT.
FORBIDDEN: no mirrored or flipped composition, no repositioning of either figure, no key from the right, no camera move of any kind.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE OFFER` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-14](../shots/scene-11/14_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
She raises the forearm because he is waiting for it. Nothing is said and nothing is signalled: the shot begins with the request already made.
The movement is SLOW and steady — she is holding a sleeping newborn with the other arm and does nothing quickly. Her left forearm comes up from below the frame into the lower middle of the shot, inner side up, <<<birthcode>>> facing the camera side, and stops. She holds it out toward him at roughly the height it will be worked at.
It is neither a plea nor a defiance. She presents the arm the way she would present a document.
Her body does not move with it: her torso does not turn, her shoulders do not shift, her other arm keeps the child, her rocking does not change, her head does not move and her eyes stay up on his face.
The arm is held out by her own muscle and it tires: it shakes very faintly and drops a centimetre and is lifted back before he takes it.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE GRIP AND THE SCAN` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-14](../shots/scene-11/14_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
The mechanics follow <<<image_3>>> exactly.
At 0:04 his gloved hand comes into the lower frame from the right and closes around her wrist, fingers wrapping from underneath, thumb across the top, above <<<birthcode>>>. It closes ONCE, firm and settled: no regrip, no adjustment, no tightening, no thumb movement, no stroking. It is a hand holding a limb still for a machine and nothing else.
The forearm goes still in the grip, the fine tremor of holding it up replaced by the stillness of being held. He rotates it a few degrees inner-side-up, by feel, to bring <<<birthcode>>> square to the reader. That rotation is driven from outside: the arm turns as something turns it, arriving and stopping without follow-through.
At 0:05 his other hand brings <<<prop_scanner>>> in from the right and holds it a short distance above the skin at the <<<image_3>>> angle and height.
THE SCAN RUNS FOR THREE FULL SECONDS, 0:05 to 0:08. In those three seconds nothing else happens: the scanner is held still over the same patch of skin, the wrist is held still, both bodies hold, nobody moves, nobody speaks, nobody looks at anything different. The scanner does not sweep along the arm, does not pass back and forth, does not tilt, does not lift and return.
He does not raise the arm to his own eyeline. He does not turn it to look at it. He does not check a result.
At 0:08 IT ENDS. <<<prop_scanner>>> is taken away first, withdrawing out of frame to the right, and then the gripping hand simply opens. Not lowering her arm, not placing it down, not pushing it away. Her forearm falls away under its own weight, out of the bottom of frame, and does not come back.
He does not hold on afterwards, does not squeeze before releasing, does not pat the arm, does not touch her again. His hands withdraw out of frame to the right and do not return.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE FACE` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-14](../shots/scene-11/14_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
Her face is the <<<image_1>>> face and it does not change for a single frame of this take: pale, drawn, exhausted, the mouth closed and still, the expression fixed. It is identical at 0:00, while she offers the arm, while he holds it, across the three seconds of the scan, and after the release.
Physically present and required: pallor under a faint damp sheen on the forehead, the nose and the upper lip; fine loose hair at the temple and along the jaw; breathing shallow and high in the chest, visible in the nostrils and at the open throat; a fine involuntary tremor at the jaw and in the muscle beside the mouth, small enough to almost miss; eyes locked high and not blinking longer than is comfortable; the eyes wet with strain, but no tear forms and nothing runs.
NOTHING ARRIVES ON THIS FACE at any point. No reaction when his hand closes, no reaction to the scanner, no reaction to the completion, no release of tension when he lets go, no exhale that reads as relief.
NOTHING READABLE PASSES OUT OF IT: no plea, no appeal, no apology, no defiance, no anger, no submission, no attempt to communicate.
Of him, only the beard, chin and closed mouth are visible, and they do not change either: no jaw set, no lip movement, no tightening, no speech.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up

````text
Her head holds one angle for all ten seconds and is never frozen. Her rocking of the child travels up through her body and moves her head with it: a small slow oscillation, unchanging in speed and amplitude from the first frame to the last, continuing through the offer, the grip, the scan and the release. It never slows, never quickens, never pauses, and is never used as a reaction. Her shallow high breathing moves the head faintly on top of that, the two motions overlapping without falling into rhythm. There is a fine unsteadiness in the neck from holding the head back at a strained angle while carrying the child's weight: tiny corrections, late, overshooting by a hair and settling. Her eyes hold their high off-frame point through all of it, compensating exactly as real eyes do on a moving head.
Her held forearm keeps a fine tremor inside his grip across the three seconds, and moves slightly with her rocking. This is involuntary and is not a gesture.
His standing column carries the slow low-frequency sway of a loaded body: the plate carrier lifting and settling with each breath, the ID placard swinging a millimetre, the slung <<<m7>>> moving with the chest as one mass, the chin and mouth at the top-right moving a hair with it. His working hands are steady in a way the rest of him is not.
FORBIDDEN: no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no head movement that lands on an event, no movement timed to the scanner. No looping cycle, no smooth sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen head, no snapping between poses.
````

## scene-11-14 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE BACKGROUND IS A BARE PALE WALL AND NOTHING ELSE.` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

FOCUS NEVER MOVES and she is sharp from frame one: no rack to the arm, the grip or the scanner.
The camera does not react to anything: no pan, no tilt, no push-in, no reframe, no rack focus, no change of height.
NO LIGHTING CHANGE except the faint contained scanner lift on the underside of the forearm from 0:05 to 0:08. NO RED LIGHT ANYWHERE IN THE FRAME.
No readable text anywhere in frame: no signage, no labels, no on-screen text, no legible writing on the ID placard, no interface.
Real-time motion for all 10 seconds. No slow motion, no speed ramp, no time stretch, no freeze.
Handheld for the whole take, never locked off and never stabilised.
The take ends on her face, her eyes still up on him, her arm back at her side, the scan done, nothing said, nothing changed.

## scene-11-15 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`THE ACTION, STEP BY STEP — THE SPINE OF THE SHOT` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-15](../shots/scene-11/15_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
Everything below happens in the LOWER MIDDLE OF FRAME, in the gap between her chest and his body. It never rises toward her face and never touches the top of frame.

STEP 1 — SHE RAISES THE FOREARM (0:02 to 0:04).
Her right arm keeps holding <<<char_baby>>> against her chest and never moves. Her LEFT arm comes up alone.
The elbow stays low, near her body. Only the forearm swings up from below the bottom edge of frame into the lower middle of the shot, rotating as it rises so that the INNER SIDE finishes facing up and slightly toward the camera side.
It stops at roughly the height of her own chest, held out toward him, forearm close to horizontal, hand relaxed and open, fingers loose, wrist neither flexed nor stiff.
Her torso does not turn, her shoulders do not shift, her head does not move and her eyes stay up on his face.

STEP 2 — THE CUFF SLIDES BACK AND UNCOVERS THE WRIST (during the same movement).
The padded jacket sleeve is oversized and its cuff sits over the back of her hand at the start of the take.
As the forearm rises and rotates, GRAVITY TAKES THE CUFF: the heavy sleeve slides back down the forearm by several centimetres and bunches in a thick fold near the elbow. She does not push it back with her other hand — she cannot, she is holding the child — and she does not shake it back. It moves because the arm turned over and the fabric is heavy.
This uncovers the wrist and the lower inner forearm, and with it the birth code, which was hidden under the sleeve until now. THE BIRTH CODE IS A MARK IN THE SKIN, IN THE POSITION SHOWN IN <<<video_1>>>: matte, flat, old, with the skin texture visible over it. It never glows, never reacts to the scan, never changes, and is never rendered as a graphic overlay.
The cuff stays bunched where it landed for the rest of the take and does not slide down again.

STEP 3 — HE TAKES THE WRIST WITH ONE HAND (0:04 to 0:05).
ONE gloved hand comes into the lower frame from the right and closes around her wrist, at the position and in the form shown in <<<video_1>>>: fingers wrapping from underneath, thumb laid across the top.
IT CLOSES ON THE WRIST JOINT ITSELF, BELOW THE BIRTH CODE, so the mark and the skin around it stay completely uncovered and clear for the reader. His hand never sits on the code, never covers it, never obscures it.
It closes ONCE, firm and settled: no regrip, no adjustment, no tightening, no thumb movement, no stroking. It is a hand holding a limb still for a machine and nothing else.
The forearm goes still in the grip — the fine tremor of holding it up replaced by the stillness of being held — and he rotates it a few degrees further inner-side-up, by feel, to square the code to the reader. That rotation is driven from outside: the arm turns as something turns it, arriving and stopping without follow-through.
He does not lift the arm toward himself. He does not pull it. The arm stays where she offered it and he works at that height.
````

## scene-11-15 · Close-up. The woman sits on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms, her head up 

`STEP 4 — HE BRINGS THE SCANNER WITH THE OTHER HAND (0:05).` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [scene-11-15](../shots/scene-11/15_close-up-the-woman-sits-on-the-edge-of-the-bed-wit.md)

````text
His OTHER gloved hand comes in from the right holding <<<prop_scanner>>> and brings it over the exposed inner forearm, stopping at the height and angle shown in <<<video_1>>>: held a short distance ABOVE the skin, not touching it, its reading face parallel to the forearm and centred over the birth code.
Both hands are now in the lower frame and both stay there: one closed on the wrist, one holding the scanner above the code. The two hands are steady in a way the rest of him is not.

STEP 5 — THE SCAN (0:05 to 0:08, THREE FULL SECONDS).
The scanner is held STILL over the same patch of skin for three seconds, in the held position shown in <<<video_1>>>. It does not sweep along the arm, does not pass back and forth, does not tilt, does not lift and return, does not circle.
Nothing else happens in those three seconds. The wrist is held, the scanner is held, both bodies hold with only involuntary micro-motion. She does not look down. He does not look up. Nobody speaks. No third movement enters.

STEP 6 — HE LETS GO (0:08 to 0:09).
<<<prop_scanner>>> is taken away FIRST, lifting straight off and withdrawing out of frame to the right.
THEN the gripping hand simply opens. Not lowering her arm, not placing it down, not pushing it away, not guiding it.
Her forearm falls away under its own weight, out of the bottom of frame, and does not come back. Both his hands withdraw out of frame to the right and do not return.
He does not hold on afterwards, does not squeeze before releasing, does not pat the arm, does not touch her again.
````

IT IS NOT A STARTLE. No snap, no jerk, no whip round, no flinch preceding it, no shoulders jumping. Her body does not move: her torso does not rise, her spine does not straighten, her shoulders do not square, her arms do not tighten on the child. She does not get up. ONLY THE HEAD COMES UP.

It does not overshoot and it does not settle back. It arrives and stops, held, with only involuntary micro-motion after.
Her loose hair moves with delay and settles late; the strands falling across the side of her face trail behind the movement and come to rest across her cheek and jaw.
The rocking of the child does not pause, slow or quicken during the lift. The two motions are independent.

Her face is already turned toward him before it starts, so nothing rotates: the head goes back on the neck, the chin travelling straight up, the throat opening and lengthening, and the eyes climb from low in front of him to his face far above her. It ends exactly in the head position of <<<image_1>>>.
IT IS A LONG WAY UP, visibly greater than a level look would need, because he is standing and she is seated.
IT TAKES ONE AND TWO TENTHS OF A SECOND OF REAL TIME, ROUGHLY THIRTY FRAMES AT 24FPS, AND IT IS CONTINUOUS. That is the actual duration of the movement at 1x, not a slowed version of a faster one and not a stretched version of a shorter one. It is the ordinary speed of a head coming up in front of someone who is already there. One movement, no stages, no pause halfway, no false start. IT IS NEVER PLAYED IN SLOW MOTION AND NEVER RAMPED.
IT IS NOT A STARTLE. No snap, no jerk, no whip round, no flinch preceding it, no shoulders jumping. Her body does not move: her torso does not rise, her spine does not straighten, her shoulders do not square, her arms do not tighten on the child. She does not get up. ONLY THE HEAD COMES UP.
HER EYES DO NOT LEAD THE HEAD. She does not look up first and lift the head after, and she does not roll her eyes up under lowered brows. The gaze travels with the face and ARRIVES ON HIS FACE AT THE VERY END of the movement.
HER EYELINE STAYS ON THE SAME VERTICAL LINE THROUGHOUT: it starts low in front of him and travels straight up his body to his face. It does not swing across, does not pass over the room, does not leave screen-right at any point in the travel.
The face rises into the key from screen-left and becomes legible for the first time in the shot: the brow, then the eyes, then the full face, then the catchlight. Because the camera is above her, the face is still seen from a high angle even at the top of the movement.
It does not overshoot and it does not settle back. It arrives and stops, held, with only involuntary micro-motion after.
Her loose hair moves with delay and settles late, at real-time speed; the strands falling across the side of her face trail behind the movement and come to rest across her cheek and jaw. The hair never floats, never fans out, never drifts — that look is overcranking and it is forbidden.
The rocking of the child does not pause, slow or quicken during the lift. The two motions are independent.
FORBIDDEN DURING THE LIFT: any slow motion, any speed ramp, any easing of the footage, any stretching of the movement to fill time; no gasp, no intake of breath, no blink, no dramatic reveal, no rise timed to a sound, no eyes opening as if waking, no hair pushed back, no expression changing as the face comes into view, no lifting of the body with the head, no lateral turn of the head, no glance at the child on the way up, no second raise afterwards, no lowering afterwards.
````

FORBIDDEN: any slow-motion blink, any stretched or lingering lid movement; no blink at the head of the take, no blink during the lift, no blink as the eyes arrive on him, no blink in the last second, no second blink, no blink flurry, no double blink, no rapid fluttering, no rhythmic blinking, no half blinks, no lids drifting down, no slow closures, no long closures, no squinting, no blinking used as punctuation, as a reaction or as a beat, no blink used to fill a pause.

## scene-11-21 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms.

````text
Her eyes are locked on his face, high above her, off to screen-right. The gaze is fixed on that single point from the end of the lift to the end of the take. It does not travel, does not search his face, does not move between his eyes, does not drop to his mouth, does not go to his hands, does not go past him.
IT READS AS A FACE, NOT A SPOT: the eyes converge for a near subject a little over a metre away and make the tiny natural adjustments of a person looking at someone — micro-saccades, a fractional settling — and they compensate exactly for her head's micro-motion so the gaze stays on him through all of it.
HER EYES STAY OPEN. Apart from the single blink at 0:03.9 the lids do not close, do not lower, do not flutter and do not tighten at any point.
SHE DOES NOT LOOK AWAY FOR A SINGLE FRAME once the gaze has arrived. She does not look back down. She does not look at the child. She does not look into the lens.
The intensity is duration, not force: the eyes are not widened, not narrowed, not staring, not glaring. They are simply not leaving him and not closing.
Her eyes are wet with strain and the lower lids are slightly raised from the upward reach, but no tear forms and nothing runs.
FORBIDDEN: any direct-to-camera look, any flicker toward the lens, any glance anywhere else, any lowering of the gaze, any vacant or unfocused stare, any moment where the eyes read as looking through him or past him, any blinking beyond the single one specified.

## scene-11-21 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms.

`THE FACE` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-21](../shots/scene-11/21_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-edge-of-the-be.md)

````text
She is frightened and exhausted. Both are present in the body and neither is played on the face.
Physically present and required: pallor under a faint damp sheen on the forehead, the ridge of the nose and the upper lip; the exhausted drawn quality of <<<image_2>>>; loose strands of hair across her temple, cheek and jaw; breathing shallow and high in the chest, visible in the nostrils and at the exposed throat above the fleece collar once it is revealed; a fine involuntary tremor at the jaw and in the muscle beside the mouth, small enough to almost miss; the mouth closed, the lips slightly parted only enough to breathe.
HER EXPRESSION DOES NOT CHANGE ACROSS THE TAKE, and above all it does not change as the face rises into view. The face that arrives at the top of the lift is the face she already had with her head down. Nothing arrives, nothing resolves, nothing escalates, nothing softens between 0:01.8 and 0:05.
NOTHING READABLE PASSES OUT OF THIS FACE: no plea, no appeal, no apology, no defiance, no anger, no submission, no attempt to communicate. She is not asking him for anything. She is waiting and she is looking.
NOTHING MATERNAL IS PLAYED. There is no tenderness toward the child on this face at any point, because the child is not where her attention is. Her attention is on him from the first frame.
````

## scene-11-21 · Close-up on the woman, seated on the edge of the bed with the newborn in her arms.

`HEAD MOVEMENT — LIVING, NOT STILL` · Close-up · Handheld · 5s

Used in: [scene-11-21](../shots/scene-11/21_close-up-on-the-woman-seated-on-the-edge-of-the-be.md)

````text
Her head is never frozen, before the lift or after it. It carries continuous involuntary micro-motion for all five seconds, at real-time speed, and that micro-motion is separate from the lift. NONE of that life is carried by the eyelids.
Her rocking of the child travels up through her body and moves her head with it: a small low-frequency oscillation at real-time speed, unchanging in rate and amplitude from the first frame to the last. It continues through the lift, riding on top of it. It never slows, never quickens, never pauses, and is never used as a reaction. It never turns her face toward the child.
Her shallow high breathing moves the head faintly on top of that, faster and less regular than the rocking, the two motions overlapping without falling into rhythm.
After the lift there is a fine unsteadiness in the neck from holding the head tilted back at a strained upward angle while carrying the child's weight: tiny corrections, arriving late, overshooting by a hair and settling. This strain is greater than it would be at a level eyeline and is visible at the throat and along the sternocleidomastoid.
Her eyes hold his position through all of it, compensating for the movement exactly as real eyes do on a moving head. THE MICRO-MOTION NEVER CARRIES HER GAZE OFF SCREEN-RIGHT, at either height.
His head and shoulder carry the same kind of involuntary motion: the low-frequency sway of a standing body under load, breathing lifting and settling the mass, at real-time speed. It is never a gesture and never a turn, and it never descends toward her.
FORBIDDEN: any of this rendered in slow motion or given a floating quality; no second lift, no lowering after the lift, no lateral turn of the head at any point, no nod, no shake, no tilt used as meaning, no head turning away, no chin lift beyond the end position, no cocking, no head movement that lands on anything. No looping cycle, no smooth sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen head, no snapping between poses.
````

## scene-12-04 · Frontal close-up on a man seated at a table in the pale mint-green room.

`THE PERSON ACROSS THE TABLE — OFF-FRAME BUT REAL` · Close-up · Locked-off · 7s

Used in: [scene-12-04](../shots/scene-12/04_frontal-close-up-on-a-man-seated-at-a-table-in-the.md)

````text
<<<CAPTAIN>>> is not alone and is not speaking to himself. SOMEONE IS SEATED DIRECTLY OPPOSITE HIM, on the far side of the same table, facing him at conversational distance — roughly a table's width away, close enough that he speaks quietly and is easily heard.
THAT PERSON IS NEVER SEEN. They are outside the frame, just past one edge. No shoulder enters the picture, no arm, no hand, no hair, no out-of-focus mass at the frame edge, no shadow of them on the wall behind him, no reflection of them on any surface. NOT A SINGLE PART OF THEM IS EVER VISIBLE.
THAT PERSON IS NEVER HEARD. They ask nothing, answer nothing, make no sound at all inside this take: no voice, no breath, no cloth, no chair, no movement.
THEIR EXISTENCE IS CARRIED ENTIRELY BY HIS EYELINE AND HIS BEHAVIOUR. Because they are seated, and the table is between them, HIS EYELINE IS LEVEL OR A DEGREE OR TWO BELOW LEVEL — the height of a seated person's face across a table, not someone standing over him and not someone below him. Because they are close, his focus is near: the eyes converge slightly, as they do on a face a metre and a half away, and not on a distant point.
THE EXCHANGE: something was put to him before this take began. The head going down, the breath and the head coming up are HIM ARRIVING AT AN ANSWER, not reacting to anything they do, since they do nothing. The single word is that answer, given to them.
FORBIDDEN: any visible part of the other person; any shadow, reflection or silhouette of them; any sound from them; any reaction from <<<CAPTAIN>>> to something they do; any eyeline that reads as looking at empty space, at a wall, at a distant point, or into the middle distance while his head is up.
````

## scene-12-04 · Frontal close-up on a man seated at a table in the pale mint-green room.

IT READS AS A FACE, NOT A SPOT. The eyes converge for a near subject and make the tiny natural adjustments of a person looking at someone: micro-saccades, a fractional settling, the small drift between one feature of a face and another.
WHEN THE HEAD GOES DOWN the eyes go with it and settle low and near, on the tabletop below the frame edge. They stay there, open, for the whole of the lowered passage, including through the breath. They do not close, do not roll up, do not search the room, do not flick sideways.
WHEN THE HEAD COMES BACK UP the eyes arrive already on the other person's face, off-axis and level, in one movement with the head. There is no searching, no hunting, no orientation beat. From that moment the eyeline is fixed on that single point to the last frame.
HIS EYES NEVER MEET THE LENS. Not at the start, not on the word, not on the breath, not on the way down, not on the way up, not on a blink, not for a single frame. The eyeline never crosses the lens axis and never drifts toward it.
Whenever his head is up he does not look away: no glance up, no look to the far side, no scanning the room, no eyes sliding off at the end of the word.
His head stays square and level whenever it is up: he does not turn it toward the eyeline, does not tilt it, does not cock it. The offset is carried entirely by the eyes within a frontal head. The head drop and the head lift are straight down and straight up in the sagittal plane, with no turn and no tilt to either side.
FORBIDDEN: any direct-to-camera look, any flicker toward the lens, any moment where the eyes pass across it while moving, any head turn that would bring the gaze onto the axis, any vacant or unfocused stare, any eyes closing on the head drop or on the breath.

## scene-12-04 · Frontal close-up on a man seated at a table in the pale mint-green room.

`THE WORD AND THE BREATH` · Close-up · Locked-off · 7s

Used in: [scene-12-04](../shots/scene-12/04_frontal-close-up-on-a-man-seated-at-a-table-in-the.md)

````text
He makes exactly two vocal events in the whole take and nothing else, both in the voice of <<<audio_1>>>:
THE BREATH — one audible exhale at 3:20, HEAD DOWN, eyes low, lasting about a second. It is a real breath, not a performance: air leaving the chest and the shoulders dropping with it, audible but unforced. THE MOUTH DOES NOT SHAPE A WORD ON IT. It is not a sigh of exasperation, not of grief, not of relief, not of impatience, not a huff, not a scoff, not a groan, not a shudder, not a sob, not a laugh. It carries no attitude and no comment. It is the breath of a man who has stopped holding one.
THE LINE — "No." from 5:30, spoken AS THE HEAD COMES UP and completed once it is level, delivered to the person opposite. FIRM. Level, low, certain and unraised: the firmness is in the steadiness, not in volume or force. It is a refusal, and it is final without being hard. No stress, no hardening into anger, no threat, no plea, no crack, no tremor, no emotion played on top of it, and no rise at the end. It is one syllable and it is not drawn out.
IT IS THE ONLY WORD SPOKEN IN THE TAKE. Nothing precedes it and nothing follows it. He does not repeat it, does not add anything, does not trail off.
IT IS QUIET — the volume of two men at one table. Not shouted, not hissed, not growled, not whispered theatrically.
LIP SYNC: the face is frontal and close, so the articulation must be exact and unmistakable. On the breath the lips part only slightly and form no consonant and no vowel shape. On "No." the lips round and open once, the jaw drops a small amount, and the mouth returns to a closed neutral shape. The beard moves with the jaw. It must read as his and not as something said off-frame.
````

NO BLINK ACROSS THE WORD, none across the breath, and none in the last third of a second before either starts or the first third of a second after either ends. No blink on the head drop and no blink on the head lift: he does not close his eyes to go down and does not open them to come up.
HIS EYES ARE OPEN FOR THE WHOLE TAKE except for those three blinks. He never sits with his eyes shut, and above all he does not close them on the breath.
No blink flurries, no double blinks, no rapid fluttering, no rhythmic blinking, no blink used as punctuation or as a beat.
Between blinks the eyes stay open and wet, without the strained look of eyes forced open.

## scene-12-04 · Frontal close-up on a man seated at a table in the pale mint-green room.

`BODY LIFE — HE IS SITTING, NOT POSING` · Close-up · Locked-off · 7s

Used in: [scene-12-04](../shots/scene-12/04_frontal-close-up-on-a-man-seated-at-a-table-in-the.md)

````text
He must read as a real body occupying a chair for seven seconds, never as a portrait with a moving mouth. All of the following is involuntary, continuous and unremarkable, and none of it is a gesture or a reaction.
BREATHING drives the frame from the first frame to the last: a slow uneven cycle lifting and settling the shoulders, the collar and the shirt across the chest, visible while his head is up and while it is down. Across seven seconds he takes roughly three unequal breaths; ONE OF THEM IS THE AUDIBLE EXHALE AT 3:20, which is simply deeper and louder than the others and is part of the same cycle, not a separate event grafted on. The intervals are not metronomic and the cycle never syncs to the word, to the head drop, to the head lift or to a blink.
THE HEAD is a real mass on a real neck. Whenever it is up it carries a slow low-frequency drift of a degree or two, arriving late, overshooting by a hair and settling, continuously and never repeating. Whenever it is down it still carries that drift, smaller, and it dips a fraction further on the audible exhale and recovers. Outside the one scripted drop and the one scripted lift it never becomes a nod, a tilt or a turn.
THE TORSO makes the small postural corrections of a seated body: an almost imperceptible settling of weight, the spine adjusting a millimetre, one shoulder easing and returning. Once, around 6:30, there is a SINGLE tiny weight shift — the kind a body makes without noticing after a while in a chair — read only as a small settle through the shoulders and a shift of the shirt creases. It is not a lean, not a reposition, and it is not timed to anything.
THE SHOULDERS are not symmetrical and not locked: the right one sits lower because of the forearm on the table, and the trapezius on that side stays relaxed while the left does not. Both move with the breath, slightly out of step with each other, and both drop visibly on the audible exhale. They round very slightly forward while the head is down and return as it comes up.
THE FACE has continuous minor life beneath the fixed expression: the small tensions and releases around the eyes and mouth that a resting face always has, the nostrils moving faintly with the breath and more openly on the exhale, the throat moving with it above the collar. It is never a static mask and never a mask that swaps between poses.
HAIR AND CLOTH lag: the loose strand falling past the temple swings forward as the head goes down and back as it comes up, settling late both times; the linen at the shoulders and chest moves a fraction behind the body, never in perfect step with it.
FORBIDDEN: no looping cycle, no smooth sinusoidal drift, no CG head-bob, no locked-off frozen figure, no snapping between poses, no movement that lands on the word, no fidgeting, no restlessness, nothing that reads as an intention.
````
