````text
<<<captain-agentv2>>>: adult male tactical operative, dark olive-green combat suit, segmented chest armor plate, heavy tactical vest with faded stenciled unit codes, black sealed composite helmet with opaque black visor — face never visible, the visor a flat black void catching the ceiling lights as a sliding highlight. Lower jaw a mechanical mask of overlapping vent plates with central audio receptor, thin cable bundle hanging from the helmet base. Black tactical gloves. Automatic rifle held in both hands. He is POINT MAN, front of the stack, nearest the lens. 100% matches the reference.
THREE ADDITIONAL OPERATIVES: identical gear, identical helmets and opaque visors, identical rifles — visually indistinguishable from <<<captain-agentv2>>> except by position and build. #2 close behind his right shoulder. #3 behind his left shoulder. #4 rear, hindmost, turning to check behind the stack. All four fully clothed and geared identically, no unit patches, no flags, no national insignia, no legible text on any gear.
````

## unfiled-project-root-01 · Four tactical operatives advance down a Soviet-era corridor in a stacked formation, moving

`LOCATION` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-01](../shots/unfiled-project-root/01_four-tactical-operatives-advance-down-a-soviet-era.md)

````text
<<<terrazzoest>>>: Soviet-era institutional interior — rough plaster walls, institutional green lower half, faded white above, worn linoleum or concrete floor, exposed pipe runs, cold fluorescent tubes at intervals along the ceiling, frosted glass panels and closed doors set into the walls at intervals. Extend the reference into a long straight corridor: the tubes receding in perspective behind the stack, doorways passing on both sides, the far end unresolved in shadow.
````

## unfiled-project-root-07 · Extreme close-up on a dead man's face on the floor of an empty atrium.

`BODY POSITION — DERIVED FROM THE ACTION` · Extreme close-up · Locked-off · 8s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-07](../shots/unfiled-project-root/07_extreme-close-up-on-a-dead-man-s-face-on-the-floor.md)

````text
He was walking down the centre line of the checkerboard, away from the far glazed doors and toward the street doors behind camera, following his son. He was shot once from BEHIND. He fell forward on that same line, carried by his own momentum. Nobody moved him afterwards and nobody has come near him.
Therefore:
- The body lies ALONG the corridor axis, not across it. Head nearest camera, feet away toward the far end of the hall.
- He is PRONE: chest and stomach down on the linoleum. Not on his back, not on his side.
- One arm is folded under his body, trapped by his own weight.
- The other arm is stretched out AHEAD of him, along the floor, toward camera and the doors beyond it — the direction he was going. The forearm is rotated so the inner surface and <<<birthcode>>> face the ceiling.
- His head is turned to one side. The cheek and the side of the jaw rest flat on the lino, the weight of the skull pressing the cheek out of shape. The head is the only part of him that is turned.
- His legs are out of frame at the far end.
The pose reads as arrival, not arrangement: knees slightly apart where they landed, the jacket rucked up over the small of the back, the collar crushed and folded under the shoulder, one shoulder higher than the other. Nothing composed, nothing symmetrical, nothing dignified.
````

## unfiled-project-root-07 · Extreme close-up on a dead man's face on the floor of an empty atrium.

`THE EYES — OPEN, NOT LOOKING` · Extreme close-up · Locked-off · 8s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-07](../shots/unfiled-project-root/07_extreme-close-up-on-a-dead-man-s-face-on-the-floor.md)

````text
Both eyes are open and oriented toward the lens. This must read as the camera happening to be where his face is pointed, NOT as a look.
Fixed and non-converging: the two eyes are not aimed at the same point. Their axes sit very slightly apart, so nothing is being focused on. He is not looking at the camera. He is not looking at anything.
No accommodation, no convergence, no tracking, no micro-saccades, no drift, no flicker. The eyes are absolutely motionless.
Pupils slightly dilated and equal, dark and dull. The iris has lost its depth.
The corneas are beginning to dry: the surface is flat and slightly matte rather than wet and glossy, with the shine going out of it.
At most one small hard catchlight from the fluorescent overhead. Because the camera is locked and the eye does not move, that catchlight is absolutely static for the whole take — it does not travel, shift or change size.
No tears, no wetness running, no recognisable reflection, nothing behind the lens.
Do not add a soulful stare, do not add sadness, do not add a final look. There is nobody in there.
````

## unfiled-project-root-07 · Extreme close-up on a dead man's face on the floor of an empty atrium.

`STILL FRAME PREVENTION — CRITICAL` · Extreme close-up · Locked-off · 8s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-07](../shots/unfiled-project-root/07_extreme-close-up-on-a-dead-man-s-face-on-the-floor.md)

````text
The camera is locked and the subject is motionless. The risk is that this reads as a photograph or a freeze frame. It must read as a live take of a dead man.
Therefore the following must be present and moving:
- Live film grain, moving and reorganising every frame, clearly a moving-image grain structure and not a static overlay.
- Live sensor and stock noise in the shadows of the jacket and the dark depth of the hall.
- In the deep dissolved background only, a faint sense of air: nothing identifiable, no figures, no traffic, just the slightest instability in the bokeh of the distant fluorescents. Nothing in the near two thirds of frame moves at all.
Forbidden: freeze frame, still image, frozen grain, a held photograph, a locked plate with no grain movement, any time-stop effect, any suggestion the image itself has stopped rather than the man.
Nothing on his body moves. Not one hair, not one thread. Only the grain and the light are alive.
````

## unfiled-project-root-08 · Close-up on an armored officer's face, seated.

`THE REVEAL — IN TWO STAGES` · Close-up · Handheld · 15s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-08](../shots/unfiled-project-root/08_close-up-on-an-armored-officer-s-face-seated.md)

````text
This is the first time this face has been seen. It arrives in two parts and neither is dramatised.
STAGE ONE — the helmet comes off. The upper face is bare: forehead, eyes, brow, temples, damp disordered hair. The nose, mouth and jaw are still behind the olive gaiter, per <<<image_1>>>. Most of a face, and the part that is showing does not look at anything.
STAGE TWO — the gaiter comes down. The rest arrives: nose, mouth, chin, the short beard with grey in it.
Between the two stages there are three seconds with the eyes bare and the mouth still covered. That gap is the structure of the shot: the audience gets his eyes and has to wait for his mouth.
Neither stage is treated as a reveal. No slow lift held for effect, no beat of stillness as the face appears, no light change, no music, no camera move, no exhale timed to either uncovering. He simply takes off two pieces of kit and both times a bit more of him is there.
````

## unfiled-project-root-09 · A man sits on a sofa in an empty apartment, helmet already off, face bare.

`THE HANDS` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-09](../shots/unfiled-project-root/09_a-man-sits-on-a-sofa-in-an-empty-apartment-helmet.md)

````text
He keeps his gloves on. He does not remove them to touch it.
The hands work as they have all through this sequence: no tremor, no hesitation, economical. The reach is direct. The grip is a working grip — the same one he has closed on wrists and on equipment.
Do NOT make the hands tender. No stroking, no smoothing the fur, no thumb passing over the plush, no cradling, no rocking, no holding it against his chest, no adjusting it to sit better, no straightening the caught arm, no turning it to see its face properly.
He picks it up with both hands and holds it level in front of him, at roughly chest height, in the space between his body and the lens. He holds it the way you hold something you have decided to look at.
Once the hands stop, they stop. They do not tighten, do not loosen, do not move again.
````

## unfiled-project-root-09 · A man sits on a sofa in an empty apartment, helmet already off, face bare.

`WHY THE HOLD IS THE SHOT` · Medium · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-09](../shots/unfiled-project-root/09_a-man-sits-on-a-sofa-in-an-empty-apartment-helmet.md)

````text
The action takes three and a half seconds. The looking takes five. That imbalance is the entire construction.
He should have registered it and moved on. Instead the attention stays on it well past the point where anything is being learned from looking. Nothing new is being discovered in the object: he saw everything about it in the first second.
Do not fill the hold. No expression arrives to explain it. No camera move marks it. No light changes. No sound comes in. Nothing swells. He simply does not stop looking, and the shot does not cut away.
````

## unfiled-project-root-10 · Close-up on a child's teddy bear held in a man's gloved hands.

`WHY THE HOLD IS THE SHOT` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-10](../shots/unfiled-project-root/10_close-up-on-a-child-s-teddy-bear-held-in-a-man-s-g.md)

````text
There is no action in this take. There is an object, a pair of hands, and eight seconds.
Do not fill the hold. No gesture arrives. No expression arrives — and none could, because the face is soft. No camera move marks it. No light changes. No sound comes in. Nothing swells.
The only thing that happens is that the shot does not cut away from a teddy bear for far longer than a shot of a teddy bear should last. That is the entire effect and it is made of duration alone.
````

## unfiled-project-root-10 · Close-up on a child's teddy bear held in a man's gloved hands.

`THE HANDS` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-10](../shots/unfiled-project-root/10_close-up-on-a-child-s-teddy-bear-held-in-a-man-s-g.md)

````text
He keeps his gloves on. He does not remove them to touch it.
The hands are still: they hold and do nothing. No tremor, no adjustment, no regrip, no drift.
Do NOT make the hands tender. No stroking, no smoothing the fur, no thumb passing over the plush, no cradling, no rocking, no turning it, no straightening the caught arm, no pulling it closer to his chest.
They hold it the way they held wrists and equipment: a working grip on a thing.
Where glove leather meets plush, the plush is what gives. The leather creases over the knuckles and holds its shape. That asymmetry is fully resolved at this magnification and must be visible in every frame.
````

## unfiled-project-root-10 · Close-up on a child's teddy bear held in a man's gloved hands.

`THE BEAR` · Close-up · Handheld · 10s

Used in: [unfiled-project-root-10](../shots/unfiled-project-root/10_close-up-on-a-child-s-teddy-bear-held-in-a-man-s-g.md)

````text
<<<prop_bear>>> is soft, unstuffed enough to be floppy, and old. Held in two hands it keeps its shape better than it would in one, but the limbs hang under their own weight and the head lolls slightly forward on the soft neck seam.
The body is compressed where the gloves press and stays compressed. The plush bulges between the fingers. It does not spring back and it stays out of shape.
At this magnification the surface fully reads: matted flat at the belly and the muzzle where it has been held for years, still curly and fuller at the back of the head and the outer arms, individual fibres catching the cold daylight along the top surfaces. A film of household dust in the pile. Seams visible. One dark stitched eye, flat and dead, and a dark oval felt nose worn shiny in one spot from handling.
It is not clean, not new, not precious, not lit for effect.
The bear does not move on its own at any point. No animation, no life, no cute behaviour, no head tilt of its own. It is an object being held.
````
